To Manager Lee Jaheon, this was probably just another day of returning from a Darkness only to find the office packed with civilians instead of employees. Yet, he showed no reaction. So lizard-like.

The concerning part, however, was that he was alone.

‘The researchers said Team D had returned.’

...Had the assigned employees all died in an instant?

“Yes.”

The processing of the previously cleared Mermaid Graveyard Darkness took priority.

“.......”

No way.

‘Did he recognize me?’

But his gaze moved on just as quickly. Without a word, he walked straight into the center of the office and sat down on one of the meeting sofas.

...Meaning, he casually sat down in the middle of a group of blood-soaked, contaminated children without a second thought.

Even the research team stammered.

Agent Choi also seemed to be seeing him for the first time.

Well, that made sense.

Lee Jaheon—aka Employee D—had appeared later in the Darkness Exploration Records, once the logs expanded and meta-humor clearances became more acceptable.

Even the children who had regained some strength after receiving first aid were glancing at the man in the lizard mask.

It would’ve been fine if that was all.

“Excuse me, are we going to see our mom now?”

“I, I need to go to the bathroom...”

“Subin says he’s hungry!”

The children who were in relatively better condition hesitated before raising their hands and speaking up.

Even though they had suffered a horrific experience in the sparkling palace of the Dragon King, they hadn’t lost their innocence—probably because they had spent time in that fairy-tale-like hallucination.

Besides, they were still sick and injured, lying on the uncomfortable office floor. The fact that they had held out this long was impressive.

“I really have to pee...”

But this gave the researchers an easy excuse to separate the kids. Damn it.

I could already see the researchers trying to say, "We'll take them to the bathroom, we'll take them to the cafeteria," and then subtly separating them.

␣net, it has been stolen.

The Bureau agents would definitely follow them, but the problem was that splitting up meant some kids would be left behind.

‘If the agents are separated one by one, dealing with the Daydream employees will become even harder.’

The agents were already at their limit, having used up all their stamina and sustaining full-body bruises.

This was an obvious move, and even the agents seemed to realize it. Their expressions grew colder.

That meant... there was only one thing to do.

‘We have to move first.’

And the safest person to accompany the kids...

‘Only I know who that is.’

“Yeah.”

Manager Lee Jaheon.

“Do you want to go too?”

“Yeah!”

“Me too!”

Gwak Jaegang was gone, and the familiar employees had left for interviews or treatment.

On top of that, half my face was still covered in scars from ulcers and necrosis, making it unlikely that anyone would recognize me.

I moved among the children and asked Lee Jaheon, just like any other child would.

If things went south, I’d scream to signal danger.

Agent Bronze seemed to realize that I had picked the safest staff member, and though he looked reluctant, he didn’t say anything.

And so, with about six or seven children clinging to him, Lee Jaheon started walking down the hallway like a pied piper.

Fortunately, we reached the restroom without any issues.

Was he... trying to look after me because I was a kid?

Let’s move on.

A reptilian alien that fires holy bombardments probably has a different way of analyzing things...

...That meant Lee Jaheon was working alone.

Just when D-Team had been promoted to an elite squad and was about to be officially established, I—who had negotiated promotions and transfers in exchange—had been declared missing and dead.

As a result, D-Team had been left dangling, leaderless.

It wasn’t surprising that the company had been indecisive for months, unsure of what to do with the team. But...

The reality of it left a bitter taste in my mouth.

‘There must have been some rumors.’

Even if Lee Jaheon wasn’t the type to care about such things.

A strange phrasing.

Click!

Gwak Jaegang.

Children are quick to pick up on the atmosphere. The moment the fear spread, the rest hesitated, stepping away from the intimidating man in the white coat and crowding behind the kinder-looking uncle.

"?"

Creak.

Reinforcements had finally come.

Things went more smoothly than expected after that.

As soon as the Bureau received Agent Choi’s emergency message, they arrived at Daydream Corporation with rescue vehicles in tow, fully prepared to escalate the situation.

"We’ll just collect some simple samples. That’s harmless, isn’t it?"

Agent Bronze nearly exploded, but in the end, they allowed the Bureau to take biological samples from the mermaid juveniles...

‘They only scraped from the severed tails.’

It didn’t harm the kids.

The only worrying part was that they took a piece of my flesh too... I’d need to contact Director Ho quickly to deal with that.

"Wow!!"

We were finally free from the ghost story.

The children had passed out.

After being transported in the Bureau’s rescue vehicles, they were taken straight to headquarters, where they were bathed, treated by medical professionals, changed into clean clothes, and given food.

The same went for the agents.

For the first time in forever, they finally got to shower in hot water and eat a meal.

Me as well.

I slumped onto the sofa, feeling at ease with my newly recharged goblin-fire prosthetic arm and fully restored adult body.

For some reason, the standby room of Team Hyunmu 1 felt ridiculously comfortable.

"You never know what tricks that company is pulling."

Huh?

No—more precisely, at the still-visible ulcer scars covering half of my face.

Right. Even though I had returned to my original body, the traces of the infection hadn’t disappeared.

"......!"

Chapter 165

The Elder.

The leader of Team Hyunmu 1.

The one who had granted the agents a goblin trick through that window in the lounge, allowing them to enter the shimmering palace of the Dragon King as children.

So, I assumed we’d go through some magical ritual at that very window to visit the Elder.

But that’s not what happened.

Click.

A nursing hospital.

Traces of people.

[Do You Know About the Red Clothes at Exit 4?]

An internet-originated ghost story.

Modern internet is practically an incubator for urban legends.

All kinds of rumors, gossip, and ghost stories circulate freely, with the truth and falsehoods blending together, spreading from person to person, no, from post to post, even from video to video.

People guide the stories in the direction that aligns with their own values.

To the way I find the most interesting, fun, and plausible.

And in the process of changing and regenerating, some of these stories end up with incredible staying power.

Even though they clearly started as lies.

For example....

The ghost story I’m investigating right now.

[Do You Know About the Red Clothes at Exit 4?]

(Photo)

It seemed effective, as some troublesome guests were being turned away at the entrance.

‘Good.’

The yellow mascot sighed in relief, thinking he was finally getting the hang of how to manage the resort as it entered its second day.

But, just before closing time that day...

An unexpected incident began to unfold.

I looked at the employee candidate... no, the hopeful guest lacking funds.

With dull eyes, still appearing human, the person anxiously searched their pockets, trembling.

“Ah, I don’t have any... I don’t have any money...!”

Oh!

I extended my hand in a welcoming gesture and kindly spoke.

Then.

Work.

Labor is also counted!

“Eek!”

I hoped they would leave the theme park before business hours ended. I glanced at my front paw, tasting the air.

‘Finding the last one... difficult.’

Failure to recruit someone in exchange for accommodation fees.

Still, I thought maybe one person would eventually be caught.

‘...How much longer?’

It might take longer than I thought, and I started to feel a bit anxious, but since this was not a situation where I should panic, I kept it under control.

I am a mascot.

-See you next time~

The theme park’s business day ended.

Second day concluded.

A bloody creature with human limbs growing instead of arms.

...It might have seemed like a gruesome sight.

The cleaning employee, a rat mascot, hurriedly covered their mouth. Pure fear, coming from the threat to survival rather than supernatural terror.

Huh?

It wasn’t fear, but a strange emotion rising.

Discomfort.

‘You shouldn’t cut in line.’

Isn’t that rude?

And why, so blatantly dirtying the floor like this?

It’s undignified.

It doesn’t fit our resort.

I, along with the standard mascots, stared at it.

Dolphin mask.

Shouldn’t he have left yesterday? Why is he still here, and...

‘How did he survive last night?’

In a theme park that had closed for business.

Dragging two dead bodies.

I barely confirmed the horrific appearance of the bodies in Lee Seonghae’s hands.

Masks and formal suits.

They were the teammates who had drawn red cards, just like Assistant Manager Lee Seonghae.

A chill ran down my spine.

‘They were dead, and he was dragging them around.’

When physical distance was too far, they died by strangulation.

I felt something was off, but as a mascot, I had to kindly attend to him.

Accommodation.

“Ah.”

Thud.

Assistant Manager Lee Seonghae dropped the bodies carelessly on the floor and handed me something small, golden, covered in blood.

It was solid gold.

It seemed like something originally intended to be decoration, broken apart.

As a mascot, I couldn’t... sigh.

No.

Ah.

Work?

This might work after all.

Assistant Manager Lee Seonghae’s lips parted...

Wow!

Ah.

Right. For the employee, that’s the most important part of the contract.

We made a resort and started regular operations in just half a day after everything was in ruins.

The employee is entitled to housing during the time they work at this resort.

Hmm.

Employee: Dolphin

...It was like a lie.

That’s a relief.

It’s to ensure he can work properly.

Good.

Assistant Manager Lee Seonghae is trying to drag the bodies again. Aaah!

No!

The uniform will get dirty!

Leave it.

In the resort.

It’s fine.

So, when they’re working as employees, they seem to be recognized as a group within the resort.

And as for the bodies from my team... hmm. They’ve automatically become part of my crew.

Now, they’re mine.

...Let’s not worry about what happens next. It’s mentally unhealthy, and I don’t have the luxury to focus on those things right now.

Let’s take care of the living.

Rest.

Thank you.

It looked like the dolphin was cheering.

Spa ○

Restaurant

The restaurant is a staple in ghost stories, the fitness center has a high gore risk, and the swimming pool overlaps with the blue zone, so I excluded them.

Let everyone enjoy a sauna and massage!

Chapter 175

The morning of the third day since the opening of the Flower Golden Resort arrived.

Over there.

The mascot pointed to the left window of the resort.

Beyond that window, part of the resort zone, which was still a ruin, was visible.

At that moment,

"Operating!"

And the moment the mascot declared it...

"...!!"

The ruins beyond the window began to reconstruct.

Shattered glass swirled and formed a hemispherical shape, with gold decorations sparkling in the gaps.

Then, the window was filled with vibrant golden flowers.

From above, steam emerged, and a glass passageway stretched out from the resort building, bursting through the window, creating a door that connected it.

Standard mascots rushed out and placed an elegant sign above it.

[Healing Golden Spa]

All the employees blinked, staring at the golden glass greenhouse.

At a glance, it seemed like a beautiful place right out of a fairy tale, but it wasn’t a place for people.

It was a place where, if an unsuspecting human entered to enjoy the spa without any information, they would end up with horrific endings like “■■ became missing,” or “bones of the limbs were removed, later found in a recycling bin.”

The oils used for it were like that.

The oils used in the spa were made from the finest ingredients... Sun Orange, grape, fig, apple juice from the Everlasting Farm, ■■-year-old blood of ■■, blood vessel juice from bipedal primates, etc., carefully selected.

...Kim Soleum recalled the example of the spa introduction and felt a dizzying sensation.

‘It’s perfect for death.’

So, after much contemplation, he implemented an emergency solution.

Automation!

At the Healing Golden Spa, all massages are provided by plants filled with expert-like consciousness, tailored specifically for each person.

The massages in this place were all provided by strange greenhouse facilities automatically.

Kim Soleum put coins in to sever the dangerous connection between the staff and this hazardous place as much as possible...

Though the spa became even more bizarre in the process, there was no regret!

‘No more adding Napolitan-style work rules...’

The theme "Ensuring a private, quiet rest for precious guests" worked perfectly again.

By the way, the remaining coins were gathered and used to change the bed furniture to match the luxury bedding in the resort room.

Investment without considering profitability!

‘Please stay quietly in your rooms without coming out to the lobby or halls.’

It was purely for minimizing foot traffic and blocking variables.

In essence, it was a sealing mechanism for exorcism.

The mascot’s effort to prevent the work rules from reaching the 20th iteration was heartwarming...

The yellow mascot sighed as he finally assigned new tasks to the new employees.

The ladybug mask.

‘...The useless employees are being placed where they won’t interact directly with guests...!’

In a sense, he hit the nail on the head.

From Kim Soleum’s perspective, the more reliable employees were placed to deal with the ghost stories directly.

It was a utilitarian arrangement to increase the survival rate of all employees, but naturally, people judge others based on their own values and ways of thinking.

‘He’s selecting them!’

The ladybug employee clenched his hands.

If he keeps doing this, the useless employees will either be eliminated or transformed into standard mascots, right?

Either way, it’s a cold-sweat-inducing ending.

He looked around at the competitors with dark eyes.

Since they were already employees, the "clear without being noticed" formula that would let them sneak through safely couldn’t apply anymore.

In that case, he would need to use another formula.

Get into the passable range.

Make sure the ghost story rates him as someone who can survive...!

The yellow mascot called in his team of employees and stamped their boarding tickets with the seal placed at the entrance of the spa.

Next to him, the rat employee seemed to be relaxing and even started grinning.

“If things keep going like this, we’ll be able to escape within four days?”

“......”

What a fool.

The ladybug employee anxiously thought.

Where do I stand in the order of room service?

‘I can’t be pushed aside before I get all three stamps.’

Start of business.

The day began with new facilities.

That day’s business was successful.

On the fourth day of operation.

Kim Soleum exchanged a bewildered glance with Jang Heowoon.

“......”

That’s right.

Since the spa opened, strange phenomena had been occurring.

Until yesterday, the guests who came to the resort mostly looked like they were here to enjoy the theme park, or at least looked like they had enjoyed the theme park.

It made sense. After all, this was a theme park-affiliated resort.

But today... there seemed to be an increase in strange appearances.

"I heard it’s a place where I can rest quietly. Thank you for the gift. Please don't mind me. As long as there’s bathwater, I’m fine. Thank you."

The creature that just passed by was a bizarre slime-like entity with limbs attached.

It looked like something that would appear in a ghost story about getting stranded in a secluded forest.

They didn’t have wristbands or souvenirs, nothing indicating they were here for the theme park. Clearly, they didn’t look like they came for a theme park experience.

A crowd that could almost be described as a rush!

Is this really working?

Next.

Had a familiar face.

A pale man in a uniform-like costume and a cap.

Kim Soleum broke into a cold sweat!

Why is that guy here in a theme park-like place? Was there any of the Four Horsemen of the Apocalypse who’d be interested in a theme park?

He needed to assess the situation.

Eventually, the yellow mascot spoke up.

“Hmm?”

This...

One more gift.

Kim Soleum hurriedly prepared a survey asking "How did you find us?" and handed it over.

...??

The coupons that the blue mascot had been handing out.

Flower Golden Resort

Deluxe Room 1-day stay voucher

(Review required)

The mattress is soft.‘......’

An unexpected boom!

Kim Soleum could have screamed, wondering, ‘Why is business going so well?’ and suddenly reduced the number of rooms dramatically, and no one would have found it strange.

3 employees300 satisfied guestsMascot ceremony ritualThe required guest count had increased exactly tenfold.

Assistant Manager Lee Seonghae asked as he looked at the yellow mascot, whose horns were drooping.

Good job.

The ladybug-masked employee sitting at the room service call desk.

“......”

Jang Heowoon, the employee wearing the bison mask who had been promoted to manager, was working.

That night.

“Oh, it’s me. Ladybug.”

“Ah!”

Jang Heowoon, who had been recalling various work rules, relaxed slightly, and the tension faded from his face.

‘Okay, good.’

Jang Heowoon opened the door, and the ladybug employee, with a somewhat awkward expression, stepped inside the room.

“Hello, Mr. Bison. Sorry to bother you, but could I borrow something...?”

“Uh, excuse me.”

“Yes?”

Jang Heowoon blocked his path.

“Please don’t come in.”

“......!”

“It’s in the work rules.”

Please refrain from inviting other guests into your room.Even if a fellow employee visits, it’s better not to make exceptions. Once you realize they are not yourself, it’s already too late.

“......”

“Of course.”

“My water bottle is empty. Could you just lend me a bottle of water? I’ll leave immediately...”

“Ah...! Of course. I should have extra. Please wait a moment...”

Under the bison mask, Jang Heowoon’s complexion brightened slightly as he quickly retrieved a bottle of water from the fridge inside the room.

“......”

Just a little.

Flutter.

“......”

Oddly, it felt scarier to be resting at night. Recalling the blood and intestines he had seen earlier during the day made him feel a tightness in his stomach.

This resort was truly a wonderful place, and the mascot was truly great, amazing, and magnificent.

Chapter 176

The resort must open.

Even if someone died three hours ago.

My mascot instincts identify the employee standing next to me as the being I contracted.

"Wow... thank you!"

Wait!

Take it.

Chapter 180

I remember it exactly.

So vivid it’s impossible to forget. It was a turning point in my time at the Hundred Dreams Corporation.

The summoning ritual for a Kind Friend.

Dip your finger in water and draw a hexagram based on the enclosed design on a smooth surface.I ran to the bathroom, brought back water, poured it on the resort office floor, and began drawing the diagram with my finger.

Strangely enough, I could recall the exact pattern with perfect clarity. As if someone had carved it directly into my mind.

‘...In that case!’

It didn’t need to be real silver—just a small, circular token with monetary value.

I thoroughly wiped the water off the mascot's gloved hand, then picked up the coin and placed it on the belly of the suit.

And as the mascot raised his hand in time with the music’s climax—

Aaaaaah!

His name exploded into light in the sky, solidifying full recognition.

FLOWER GOLDEN.

The master of the yellow zone of the Cheerful Theme Park.

Everyone knew. It had been proclaimed to all.

Congratulations. He was now the complete mascot of this place.

And the yellow mascot received an overwhelming new sense.

‘...!’

He could now sense the entire yellow zone.

He could feel everything happening within his resort zone, could control every mascot as if it were himself, could perceive all the rules and ████ of the Cheerful Theme Park!

He was now the complete master of the zone. The mascot!

Aaaaah!

The instinctive euphoria magnified the spectacle of the illumination, and once again, golden flowers exploded across the entire garden as the ceremony came to its radiant end.

Aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaah!

But meanwhile—

From beyond the red gate, something else began to emerge.

The parade of madness.

The grotesque, overwhelming, dazzling procession led by Magic Bunny.

[The Great Magic Bunny!]

The red rabbit, the wagon, the giant face-shaped balloon, and the various mobile parade contraptions all halted before the gate, having completed their final sacrificial ritual.

And as if to challenge the illumination itself, their lights blazed madly—

Then went dark.

At the same time, the wave of brilliant illumination faded into the final rapture and quieted.

Ending.

What followed was...

The ending theme.

A warm variation of the theme song began to play.

Closing time was near.

["Attention guests: our theme park will be closing in ten minutes...."]

The garden fell into calm.

Guests who had gathered to enjoy the illumination began to scatter, moving quickly toward the exit gates.

In an instant, the zone returned to its peaceful, orderly bustle.

End of business hours.

The yellow mascots tidied the night garden, kindly guided the guests, and managed the resort’s facilities.

Beyond the dark red gate, no movement was seen.

The yellow mascots, lightly congratulating one another on today’s event, checked the lobby to ensure no guests remained.

Beyond the dark red gate, no movement was seen.

Before long, only a gentle glow through the lobby windows remained, casting a serene light on the quiet resort building. The yellow mascots standing inside waved to guests exiting through the gates.

Beyond the dark red gate, no movement was seen.

And then the lobby doors closed.

Thud.

“See you next time~”

.......

.......

.......

Beyond the dark red gate—

Dozens of red eyes began to emerge.

The Magic Bunny parade swarm crossed the gate as it was.

Thud-thud-thud-thud!

The sound of bodies—both corpses of guests and something still living—being crushed beneath the wheels, used as a carpet so the wagons wouldn’t get caught on the gate. The gate turned red with blood.

The wagons rolled forward. From the music box device attached to the wheels, a quaint little melody chimed out. In rhythm with that tune, the ones wearing Magic Bunny suits staggered forward in a grotesque parade into the resort.

The mascot suits melted sluggishly.

No longer bound by the hour of business, the red mascots abandoned their forms to cross into another zone.

Creatures beyond the rules and regulations—no longer needed now that the park was closed.

Aaaaaah!

Bipedal red masses fused together like veins, forming a collective body. The parade, no longer hiding behind the illusion of a theme park, warped into something that couldn’t be disguised.

But the parade continued.

With the sound of the music box still echoing, they trampled across the yellow zone’s garden and surrounded the resort building in a massive parade-body swarm.

They moved in spirals, tightening slowly around the building.

Closer.

And closer.

Crushing inward.

The yellow mascots watching from inside the lobby took up defensive positions as they saw them.

They drew closer.

Aaaaah!

The lobby’s glass windows shattered as the parade surged into the resort building.

Hell broke loose.

Murderous rage and destructive madness spilled forth, unfiltered by mascot protocol. Dozens of yellow mascots were grabbed and slaughtered.

Some of the yellow mascots tried to fight back or gang up on lone attackers, sometimes managing to overwhelm a few—

But only for a moment.

They were quickly ripped apart like toys, stuffing flying, their bodies ceasing to function.

Mascots falling from rooftops, heads bursting, others nailed into the walk-in freezer.

Horrific. Brutal. Breath-stealing carnage.

Magic Bunny’s occupation.

Magic Bunny’s overwhelming power.

Thud-thud-thud-thud-thud!

From within that chaos, a single yellow mascot fled in a panic.

The one with the ornate horns.

The very mascot that led tonight’s illumination ceremony.

The original.

Kya-ha-ha-ha-ha!

Magic Bunny’s decorative fireworks exploded with laughter. The flame shot out and burned the fleeing mascot’s leg.

It fell. Crawling now.

The lead wagon of the parade charged down the narrow hall after it, bearing down like a beast.

Toward the facilities office.

Clack!

The yellow mascot slipped into a restricted space meant only for staff of the yellow zone, slamming the door shut just in time to block the wagon—

BANG!!

The gaudy decoration at the front of the massive wagon shredded the door and burst inside.

A brightly colored wagon, decked in string lights, once leading the parade.

The symbolic vehicle of Magic Bunny.

The huge red smiling face mounted on the front split open.

Thud-thud-thud-thud-thud!

Its eyes burst, its mouth cracked wide, powder burst into the air—and from within, finally, something slithered out.

Aaaaah!

The true body of Magic Bunny.

Something that had been consumed and digested by the red mascot.

A grotesque mass that had long lost all traces of its pre-mascot form spilled out from the wagon.

Attached here and there, like trophies or replacements, were various human body parts.

Countless eyes, ears, limbs.

Aaaaah!

It shuddered in delight as it approached the yellow mascot.

Slowly, slowly.

How joyous!

A hunt in the raw. Raw pleasure!

Revenge and release!

The thrill of theft!

The yellow mascot, leg crippled and leaking stuffing, could no longer run.

It could only look up—at something so terrible, so overwhelming—

And opened its mouth.

I di ot.

At that instant—

BOOM!!

With a deafening explosion, the ceiling of the facilities office caved in.

Whether the red mascot meant to devour the yellow mascot’s head didn’t matter. It wasn’t just the ceiling that collapsed.

KA-BOOM! CRASH-CRASH-CRASH!!

The resort blew apart.

Massive construction materials came crashing down onto the swarm of parade creatures chasing the yellow mascot.

Aaaaah!

A new kind of indiscriminate destruction.

But the blasts didn’t stop there.

The resort, crushed under its own weight and gravity, caved in on the first floor, collapsing downward.

Countless Magic Bunny masses were caught beneath, crushed to death.

Alongside the yellow mascots.

KA-BOOOOOM-BOOM-BOOM!!

Even the facilities office—where the largest Magic Bunny mass had emerged.

A slab of concrete and a twisted metal pipe slammed down onto the desk.

On that desk... lay a document.

I’ve decided to demolish the resort today.

Why?

Because I feel like it. Someone once advised me this resort was too modest. Seems the previous manager had no taste.

It’s better to blow it up and build something nicer from scratch.

Unfortunately, I had to refund tonight’s guests and kindly escort them to the exit.

During the mascot ceremony, I planted the explosives.

Maybe I’ll blow it up tomorrow morning to generate some buzz...

Then again, I might just blow it tonight.

Doesn’t matter.

Because I’m the true owner of the yellow zone.

And—

Everything that was still in the resort at that moment...

Should be demolished together with it.

I’ve made that my will.

The resort was now completely destroyed and burning.

Along with everything inside.

Chapter 183

Tiger-Hour Blade.

A blade said to have been forged in a ritual timed to the Tiger year, month, day, and hour—all under the Tiger sign—to repel ghosts in the past.

Especially the one now held in the hands of the Team Leader of Xuanwu Team 3 at the Bureau—this one wasn't even sharpened. It was crafted entirely as a ceremonial weapon.

But precisely because of that, it displayed unparalleled power in supernatural disasters.

"Hup."

Naturally, it couldn’t be used in just any supernatural disaster.

Nor could just anyone wield it.

—The conditions: A situation where something wicked and depraved, taking the form of a ghost or calamity, invades directly.

Only when these criteria were fulfilled to near perfection would its true power be revealed.

However, in most supernatural disasters, people usually stumbled into a haunted area or got dragged into it.

In contrast, a case where the enemy invaded was far rarer. And even then, it was uncommon for the threat to be judged as entirely depraved.

But—

"Perfect."

The team leader's hand trembled as if seized by a thrill.

Because the target had invaded a zone she herself managed as an employee.

And, because it had cast off the mask of the friendly theme park mascot!

The pieces fit together like a perfect puzzle, a rush of exhilaration rising in her chest.

"Ha!"

Thus, the pure, tiger-aligned energy that surged along a half-formed sword sign became lightning and struck down the depraved and wicked entity.

With overwhelming force.

Utterly!

Aaaaaaaah!

"ARGH!!"

The ground shook so violently the tremor reached all the way to the distant shelter. The deafening thunderclap rippled outwards in waves.

Dust and debris from ruined architecture billowed into the darkened air, obscuring vision for a moment—

"...!!"

The remains of the resort building, crumbling and ablaze.

Amidst them, for the briefest instant, the mangled corpse of the massive Magic Bunny parade float was visible, casting a shadow in the flickering flames.

And then—

KWA-KWA-KWA-KWANG!!!

Even the last remnants of the resort collapsed, and whatever had been inside the red mascot was buried deep beneath the earth, entombed along with the building.

Completely.

"......."

"......."

"I-Is it over?"

The Xuanwu Team 3 leader opened the shelter door and lightly jogged inside.

"Ack! Team Leader!"

"Quiet."

She sighed, brushed off her clothes, and, after giving the sword a focused look, gave a small nod.

"...No more depraved things here."

"Phew!"

The agents let out a collective sigh of relief.

Even the Hundred Dreams Corporation staff—who had already suspected the Bureau’s identity from their appearance and speech but hadn’t dared to make a move—let out a breath of relief.

"Was this what the mascot asked for?"

The team leader gave a subtle nod.

The employees murmured in quiet awe. So the mascot had planned all this!

Yeah. So while it had used the entire resort building as a trap to pin down the rampaging Magic Bunny, the plan was to have an appropriate agent attack—

......

Wait a minute.

"T-Then what about the mascot...?"

They got struck by the lightning too and buried inside the building.

Silence.

Everyone turned to look at the team leader, but she simply gave a small shake of her head.

"No word about that part."

"......!!"

Wait.

"I-I mean, if even one yellow mascot’s still around, couldn’t we talk to them? It looked like they could switch bodies...."

"They were all left behind at the resort!"

Besides, they’d all seen it from the shelter—the crazy Magic Bunny hunting down the very last yellow mascot, chasing it down in that wild parade!

"Then... the yellow mascots are all dead too?"

"...!"

Some of the staff began to panic at the agent's speculation.

Field Mouse grabbed the ex-ladybug staff member by the collar.

"Aaaagh!"

"Easy for you to say! I drew the yellow horse, so I have to drag the mascot’s body out too! I can’t leave this zone otherwise!"

The stronger the mascot got, the more freely one could move inside the zone, but that freedom still came with restrictions.

To clear the board game without getting strung up, you had to stick together!

...With a dead mascot?

Field Mouse—the only one from the Yellow Horse team who hadn't died—went pale.

Tap, tap.

A polite knocking sound.

A figure in a bizarre mask, black smoke billowing from their body.

Bizarre. Like a human form chained into a belt—the Security Team employee.

The true form that had been inside the yellow mascot suit.

He’s alive!

Right. After the park closed, the yellow mascot had taken off the costume, just like the Magic Bunny.

Totally fine!

The main body of the yellow mascot, having quietly turned to look at the weasel mask, gave a small nod.

At that moment, the entire shelter filled with relief.

They were alive!

They had survived the Magic Bunny’s assault!

"Aaaaah!"

And watching it all quietly was the team leader from the Supernatural Disaster Management Bureau.

‘Hmm.’

Her eyes swiftly scanned the yellow apparition.

So, that must be the true form that had been hiding inside the cute yellow mascot costume.

‘Did Hundred Dreams really learn how to control supernatural disasters?’

They’d been obsessed with how the Bureau cooperated with goblins, trying to figure out how it was done, and now it seemed they may have developed their own method.

‘...That thing looks like it’s been sealed with something like a geas too.’

It didn’t look easy to deal with, but not all powerful things were evil.

She realized that this being had shown genuine goodwill toward the human employees and had tried to protect them.

What if Magic Bunny had realized the yellow mascot’s true body was hiding in the shelter?

It would have attacked the entire shelter—killing all the staff for sure.

The team leader felt a strange unease, but brushed it aside for now.

At any rate, that overwhelming supernatural presence appeared to hold enough goodwill that it couldn’t be clearly judged a disaster.

The mood had grown surprisingly relaxed.

Back when it was still the yellow mascot, its sheer presence had been enough to make her shudder.

The team leader conveniently interpreted it as a costume change!

Chapter 184

I moved forward slowly.

Magic Bunny’s Fantasy Land.

Everything had come to a stop, and it was dark.

A silent amusement park was already frightening enough just in concept to stir all kinds of dreadful thoughts.

Especially when I was the one walking at the very front.

Huuuu....

Was this what it felt like to be dressed as a ghost, hiding in a haunted house?

I couldn’t tell whether I should laugh or cry at the fact that I was wearing a Security Team disguise.

What was certain was that the strange sense of comfort the mascot costume had given me was gone.

...It had its pros and cons, but the rising tension was unavoidable.

[Ah, a midnight infiltration. Truly a thrilling and delightful scenario!]

[And I simply must say this one more time—what a spectacle earlier! A colossal trap hunting a colossal monster! Destruction really is one of the most primal joys. What an entertaining show it was!]

Hmm.

I heard Brown’s voice over the receiver with his usual cheer, and I felt a faint sense of relief.

‘Should I be glad at least he’s enjoying himself?’

If I’d promised the host of a ghost story that things would be fun and he ended up calling it boring, I’d be breaking out in cold sweat by now.

Even aside from that, this was a situation that warranted both relief and satisfaction.

It was going according to plan.

Squelch.

Behind me, the Blue Mascot.

No, someone who had taken off the Blue Mascot costume.

Thinking of him made a chill run down my spine, but at the same time, something within me sighed in relief.

Terrifying, but also like a divine ally at my side.

‘I’m just glad we agreed before the big ceremony.’

At first, I’d wanted to ask him to launch an attack on the Magic Bunny zone the moment the theme park closed.

Strike the Red Zone while my resort was under attack—have him devour it too.

But the timing might’ve gone wrong... and if the Blue Mascot had been overwhelmed, it would’ve been a disaster.

‘Their power seems pretty evenly matched.’

The Blue Mascot probably thought Magic Bunny was just as strong as he was, which is why they maintained mutual avoidance.

Touching the balance could mean mutual destruction.

‘Judging from the fact that crazy Red Mascot never invaded the Blue Zone, there must be some power equilibrium going on.’

That’s why I had to set the stage so he could devour Magic Bunny safely.

‘Step by step.’

And so, the most conservative course, statistically speaking, led to this moment now.

I and the employees hid in the shelter, then bombarded the resort itself.

And now, we’re invading the Magic Bunny’s zone with the formidable Blue Mascot in tow.

Of course—

There was a condition.

DO NOT

LOOK

‘.......’

Even so, I couldn’t help but listen.

Squelch.

The sound of water splashing behind me. The sound of a tail dragging across the ground. The sound of something massive pulling itself forward with belly and feet.

I heard it.

It sounded like a tidal wave, or a serpent sliding, or perhaps like wings or whiskers brushing the floor. Maybe something oozing, melted into the ground.

Following behind—something that had removed the Blue Mascot costume.

Squelch.

My nerves were on edge, instinctively alert.

Maybe... unlike Magic Bunny, the Blue Mascot hadn’t been completely devoured by the park and stripped of its outer form.

It still retained a semblance of shape.

Of course, I couldn’t turn around to confirm it.

‘There’s a reason for that.’

I left the smoke-script “Do Not Look Back” floating overhead without erasing it. Oh yeah, I’d somehow been able to start using this recently. Maybe the costume affected me in reverse?

In any case, we continued to advance.

Our destination was the center of the Red Zone.

Magic Bunny’s Fantasy Castle.

"......."

"......."

Even though I hadn’t told anyone to keep quiet, even the civilian rescue targets from the Bureau held their tongues, stepping carefully.

‘Not that it’s really necessary...’

But maybe out of anxiety, our steps gradually turned into something close to a jog.

The brightly colored amusement park palace loomed rapidly out of the darkness.

The largest, central facility in this zone.

‘...There’s definitely something left behind.’

Even if Magic Bunny’s main body and minions had attacked the Yellow Zone, been struck by lightning, and buried under rubble, there were still scraps left in the Red Zone.

And any of those scraps could become Magic Bunny again.

I know now. That’s what kind of species mascots are.

And if left alone long enough, those scraps could become a fully functional main body.

We had to catch them before that happened.

"...We’ve arrived."

I heard Assistant Manager Lee Seong-hae whisper behind me, and I came to a halt.

We could see the rear of Fantasy Castle.

Right. We had approached from behind, and the exaggerated fairy tale-style palace, like a proper theme park structure, had a cleverly disguised modern maintenance door at the back.

"......."

I didn’t stop Assistant Manager Lee Jaheon as he forced it open with brute strength.

Crack, creeeeeeak.

The door opened to reveal a bright, colorful, and eerily silent interior.

Something horrific, disguised as amusement park set pieces, appeared out of the shadows only to vanish again once my lantern light flickered out.

Gulp.

Someone behind me swallowed hard.

[Well then, search to your heart’s content, my friend...]

I thoroughly investigated every inch of the walls, moving toward the gate where the Red Bunny mascot would have stood to greet visitors.

And there, what I was looking for revealed itself.

AUTHORIZED PERSONNEL ONLY

"This is..."

"...Like a staff-only area?"

Correct.

The resort had a similar staff-only operations office.

Given I’d found operational documents and stamps there, I figured the Red Zone would have a similar space.

If there was anywhere to claim official control over the Red Zone—

‘This would be it.’

"Is this our destination?"

I nodded.

Without hesitation, Assistant Manager Lee Jaheon forced open the locked door once more.

A staircase leading downward appeared—modern architecture, rusted as if corroded by blood in various places.

"......."

I used smoke-script to present the others with a choice.

Choose freely

▶ Wait here

▶ Go down

"Uh...?"

Then I stepped forward alone to the stairs marked “NO ENTRY – MAGIC BUNNY ONLY.”

‘Please, let just a few strong ones come with me.’

The idea of going alone, unable to turn around, while something formerly known as the Blue Mascot silently followed behind... made me break out in a sweat.

But what was surprising was that everyone followed me.

"I-I was going to wait, but what if I accidentally looked back?!"

"In these situations, falling behind is always fatal."

"......."

Mm. A sound enough reason.

Anyway, we began exploring the darkness again.

As we descended, we passed odd doors labeled “Supply Room,” “Freezer,” and “Magic Storage,” but I didn’t touch any of them—I just kept going down.

I could sense it.

[Oh, is this the instinct of one who was once a mascot?]

Close enough.

The others followed me, careful not even to glance at the glass windows in those doors.

And finally, we reached the bottom.

A rusted plaque came into view.

Mascot Development Room

A half-opened door.

Found it.

"...!!"

I heard the staff stifle their own screams by clamping their hands over their mouths.

Of course they did. Because what was in front of them was...

[My, how avant-garde!]

In the center of the massive development room, a huge red body filled halfway with stuffing loomed before us.

Its hind legs had sharp claws, grand and menacing membranous wings, a severed tail—only the lower half of something massive remained.

"Hiiip."

Everyone froze in fear.

Then, from beside me, a bright voice chimed in.

"Oh, it’s a mascot suit!"

"...!"

"But it looks unfinished? Like, it’s only half there."

Correct.

If you looked closely at the exposed lower half, you’d see it was hollow, and clumps of severed human hair were stuffed into it like makeshift filling.

Clear signs that they had tried to create a mascot suit from scratch.

Hoo.

Using the enhanced vision from my eye-light, I inspected the lower half of that bizarre beast more closely.

It was covered in dense scales.

Human fingernails.

‘...Ha.’

And that wasn’t all.

All around the vast “development room” were parts that had been left unfinished, unable to be connected.

A yellow vertical-slit eyeball. Small fin-like membranes. Gigantic talons like a bird’s. A tongue. Flesh from a snout.

All leftovers from mascot development. The origins of the raw materials were all too easy to guess, and made my stomach churn.

It seemed they hadn’t been able to officially present or launch this thing as a mascot.

After all, this was the Red Bunny—Magic Bunny’s—zone.

That majestic and imposing form didn’t suit a brightly colored, cheerful amusement park.

The cheerful theme park surely wouldn’t have allowed it.

"Maybe they wanted to replace the bunny and use this as the new mascot?"

"......Hard to say."

I heard the agents whisper.

[A new brand image! Predictable, but classic thinking. What’s your take, Mr. Nohru?]

Hmm.

‘...What if it’s the opposite?’

[Oh?]

‘Maybe... they were trying to recreate their original form using that suit.’

Magic Bunny had completely lost the shape it once had, swallowed by the mascot costume.

‘So maybe it tried to remake the suit to match its true self.’

Whether out of instinct or calculation.

In the end, it had failed.

‘And judging by the pieces we saw earlier, Magic Bunny’s original form was...’

Just as I reached that thought—

Something moved.

“Aaack!”

I immediately grabbed the civilian by the nape and threw them toward the agents.

Chiiirrr...

What the civilian had been staring at so intently—the tail covered in scales—was twitching, dripping with fluid.

“Haa...”

A leftover of Magic Bunny.

It had latched onto the inner wall of a tail-shaped mascot suit and was squirming.

No limbs.

None of the organs or limbs it had pieced together from missing people and park visitors.

Just the remnants of the interior—the gutted contents of a mascot suit that had lost all form.

But its cunning and aggression were still intact.

And then—

Creak creak creak creak.

The sound began echoing from all around.

All the mascot parts in the development room were vibrating.

And the huge central piece began twisting its joints, following the tail.

From beneath the red suit, deeper crimson things bulged and twitched.

“Aaaagh!”

Some screamed. Others stumbled at the base of the stairs, unable to flee because they couldn't risk turning around. The agents moved to shield the civilians—

“Should we destroy it?”

“Feels like it’s weak enough to fight now?”

No, no, that’s not it!

I shook my head rapidly and reshaped the black smoke.

I directed it toward the enormous figure that had quietly stood in a corner of the development room, following behind us all this time.

The true form of the Blue Mascot.

“...!”

The creature that had shed the Blue Mascot suit became enveloped in my black smoke, blocking it from everyone's sight for a moment.

Now!

I pointed toward the stairs, signaling everyone to evacuate.

The staff rushed toward the staircase in a flurry. I followed, positioning myself at the end, back to the door of the development room.

Standing just like the others, drenched in cold sweat, I reshaped the smoke around us once more into words:

Do not look back

And then—

Squelch.

A sound echoed from the development room.

The being inside the Blue Mascot stirred.

To fulfill its promised task.

A slow, powerful step echoed like a tidal wave. The master of the deep. A scraping sound, as if it surveyed the broken mascot suits and their contents with solemn regret.

At last, the moment had arrived.

O seed of corruption, foreign to this realm

I devour you.

“...!!”

The leftover scraps of Magic Bunny’s inner contents were crushed, drawn in as though sucked into a massive whirlpool.

Like the “bad child” who had been swallowed up at the water park.

It was consumed.

A grotesque scream. A grotesque silence.

“Huuhuhuhk...”

“Ugh!”

Everyone instinctively plugged their ears, ducking their heads—desperate not to witness something they couldn't unsee, resisting it with every fiber.

But—

I made eye contact with someone.

The figure wearing the yellow mascot mask.

Jang Heo-un was smiling faintly.

As if he had already completely submitted to something else, as if there wasn’t even a trace of self left to tremble in fear.

As if to say he’d gladly look back and witness it all, if I wanted him to.

A different kind of chill crawled over my body.

But behind us, the overwhelming presence had reached its peak.

The sound of a massive torrent battering every wall of the development room like a whip, like a tail—and then...

It disappeared.

“.......”

From behind us, a voice filled with an attempt at gentleness came through.

It is done

“.......”

Chapter 186

My second exploration of Yuquai Theme Park had finally come to an end.

And then...

The assigned research team went into a frenzy.

Exploration Results:

Out of 12 personnel deployed, 8 returned.

Among them, 2 completed the exploration within the scheduled time, while the remaining 6 were discovered in the meeting room where the board game had begun, on the sixth day.

Of these, one employee displayed symptoms of complete contamination (mask deformation confirmed). Referred to the Fox Counseling Office.

Of the 4 who didn’t return, 3 deaths were confirmed through eyewitness accounts. All were individuals who drew red tokens. One of them had only their head retrieved by a survivor, but it was lost in the Yellow Zone.

Special Note 1:

Upon visual inspection, the collected Dream Solution was determined to be of higher quality than usual. Comparable to Grade A.

Special Note 2:

Several exploration team members who returned on the sixth day testified to having worked as theme park staff. They claimed to have received items from the Gift Shop as wages.

Had this been a Wiki entry, it might have been summarized just like that—with persistent, dogged verification.

The exploration period had stretched to six times the norm, but the extracted solution had ranked one grade higher. A true irregularity.

And there was only one variable in that irregularity.

Me.

A staff member from the Special Security Department.

But even though I looked like a ghost-story entity or something tainted, I scared the research team enough to escape the interview hell and go straight home.

—"Um, thank you so much, by the way... if I wanted to treat you to a meal, where should I—"

All of this—this entire identity—is just a disguise created by Director Ho.

—"Even without that, it’s a fascinating outfit, isn’t it? I’ll extend your rental period. Who knows when you might need it again."

Because I destroyed it.

I’d been worried she’d flip over the high-grade solution, but surprisingly, she didn’t seem too attached to it.

‘...Weird.’

Either way, A-grade is A-grade, so I figured I should check the elite team’s collector as well. But for now...

...I collapsed into bed the moment I got home and slept through until the next morning.

‘I thought I was gonna die.’

The only comfort was that I didn’t wake up once all night—pure exhaustion knocked me out cold.

But if you put it another way—

...That means I lost six whole days of vacation.

This morning.

I woke up and went straight to work at the Disaster Management Bureau.

Tch.

"Whoa, long time no see. You cold-hearted MZ Podo who didn’t even call during your break..."

Agent Choi entered the Hyunmu Team 1 lounge holding an Americano and stopped when he saw me.

"Did you go train on some mountain or something? You look like you’ve been starved to death! Hey, Jaegwan, doesn’t he?"

"Huh?"

Bronze Agent, frowning as his superior dragged him forward, also flinched when he finally got a look at me.

"...Did something happen?"

I barely managed to answer.

"I... didn’t get much sleep...."

"......."

"......."

It was true.

I’d spent five nights and six days working nonstop as a mascot in a ruined resort.

Agent Choi and Bronze exchanged a glance, and as if reaching some unspoken agreement, quickly sat down beside me on the nearby couch.

"Did you not rest well?"

I looked at them with a tired, awkward smile.

Ryu Jaegwan asked cautiously:

"I heard you didn’t come in to recharge the goblin lantern during your leave. ...Are you alright?"

Ah.

"Yes. I’m fine... I must’ve forgotten since I didn’t really need it."

"......."

Sigh.

For reference, the goblin-fire barely regained its energy after a lot of coaxing and care.

It seems it got suppressed by the mascot aura or something went wrong—but after getting a recharge this morning, it returned to my arm and regained its usual cheer.

Though now, just mentioning the security suit makes it tremble and probably want to give me a big thumbs down.

"I’m sorry. You went out of your way to give me leave, and I..."

"That leave was yours to begin with, wasn’t it?"

Agent Choi’s previously playful tone softened into something more reassuring.

"Hmm... Hey, Podo. There’s a Bureau-run hot spring up at Seoraksan. Pretty nice for a welfare facility. Wanna go there next break? All of us together?"

"......."

A hot spring with coworkers?

...but once I realized which hot spring he meant, I nodded immediately.

I did want to see what kind of amenities the Bureau’s welfare facilities had.

"Yeah. Good call. ...Heh, still got some spirit in you, huh!"

"...?!"

Agent Choi ruffled my hair and patted my back.

"When you’ve been working hard nonstop, the crash after rest hits hard. Your body, your mind—everything."

"......."

"Still, you showed up for work right on time. Good job."

I came here so no one would find out I’m a spy...

Besides, after experiencing a ghost story, Daydream Corporation-style, I’ve come to realize just how tame the Bureau's ghost stories actually are...

Of course, I couldn’t say that out loud, so I simply nodded.

"Thank you."

Then I leaned against the sofa and spent the waiting time doing nothing...

Aside from desperately trying not to freak out as Agent Choi eagerly recommended we play board games, there was nothing out of the ordinary.

Thankfully, there were no callouts either.

And then, in the afternoon after lunch—

"Agent Podo!"

While delivering some paperwork, I ran into Ko Yeongeun from the new investigative unit.

"Did you hear? About the Phoenix Unit 2 agent, Hwagak, who worked with Agent Podo?"

Ah.

Ko Yeongeun didn’t seem to know about the amusement park incident yet, but she must’ve learned about Jang Heowoon’s condition through other channels.

"...No, this is the first I’ve heard. What happened...?"

Ko Yeongeun’s expression gradually grew vague, like she was trying to hold something back.

"Yesterday... he was found in a severely contaminated state, and they said he’s in isolation care. I heard he was transferred to Seoraksan..."

"......"

"They said he got caught up in a supernatu...ral disaster while on break."

"Are you talking about our rookie?"

"...!"

Ko Yeongeun and I both turned our heads at the same time. There stood the Phoenix Unit 2 team leader—Jang Heowoon’s superior—watching us with a bitter smile.

Ha...

"Yeah. New agents can get a little scared. It’s weird when your colleague suddenly stops showing up to work, right?"

"......"

"But this isn’t too bad. People find ways to carry on. The important thing is that we rescued him."

Behind the team leader’s glasses was the kind of determined will typical of agents.

But at the same time, I could sense a familiar kind of fatigue and sadness.

...As if he were remembering all the agents they hadn’t been able to return to their original forms.

"Damn."

I held in a sigh—and a curse.

At least the fact that Jang Heowoon was in a medical facility, not a glass prison, meant his identity hadn’t been exposed. That was a relief...

"Still, it’s thanks to him that we’ve got Haeggeum here!"

"Huh?"

When the Phoenix 2 team leader waved his hand, someone walking from down the hall approached us.

A team leader-level agent who had entered the theme park—the Hyunmu Unit 3’s team leader, Haeggeum.

'T-Team Leader...!'

I almost choked.

"Ah. Rookies?"

"Yeah, one of them came in with our kid... and this one’s a new recruit for Hyunmu Unit from the latest intake."

"Oh, the Destruction King!"

.......

"I’d heard how good Agent Choi is at keeping things under wraps, but this is the first time we’ve met. Nice to meet you."

"Ah, hello...!"

I nearly snapped back, but luckily held it in.

I forced a composed greeting to Team Leader Haeggeum.

Meanwhile, a memory of our ‘staff dispatch deal’ quickly ran through my head.

‘...We had to get something in return for letting Jang Heowoon go, or it would’ve looked suspicious.’

Which is why I’d received five jade tokens produced by the Bureau from that team leader.

...Recalling the wiki entry:

Jade tokens crafted by the Baridegi Atelier are often treated as currency in supernatural phenomena.

I understand they were originally made for ritual use, but I’d like to request permission for them to be included in standard agent issue.

A ghost story currency.

It probably wasn’t a big deal for them, but it was definitely useful.

"Still, we’ve got to properly greet our Destruction King new recruit. Come on, handshake."

"Y-Yes... Th-thank you."

Even without meaning to shrink back, my shoulders moved on their own. Team Leader Haeggeum gave me a light pat on the back and waved as he walked off.

I quietly let out a sigh of relief and turned back to Ko Yeongeun—

"Wait a second."

.......

"Haven’t we met before? You look kind of familiar."

Chills ran down my spine.

"Uh, I actually greeted you once in the hallway outside the waiting room... but you looked tired and didn’t notice..."

"Oh?? Sorry about that. I’ve been swamped lately. I shouldn’t blame it all on that Choi fellow. I had my chance."

Team Leader Haeggeum scratched the back of his head and laughed.

"Well, since you know, I’m the Unit 3 team leader. If you’re bored after work, drop by the waiting room sometime. Got it?"

"Th-thank you..."

Phew.

And with that, Team Leader Haeggeum walked off, chatting with the Phoenix Unit 2 team leader.

"......."

When I turned back, Ko Yeongeun was looking right at me with a face that screamed: "What the hell is going on?"

A lot’s happened, Yeongeun...

And then at 6 p.m.

I finally got off work.

‘Oh thank god.’

I was lucky to have had a daytime shift.

And the fact that there’d been no dispatch calls was fortune within misfortune. Still, even if I wanted to collapse and sleep forever, I couldn’t afford to.

‘Gotta get to work.’

"......."

I slipped a hand toward the inventory tattoo and touched the ‘thing’ I needed to handle.

The plush stuffing of a soft toy brushed against my fingertips.

Yeah.

A stuffed toy sold at the gift shop in Happy Theme Park.

I’d successfully smuggled out the original Good Friend.

I’d used all sorts of tricks to make it happen, but I’d go over those again during the ritual... What mattered was this:

Now I could summon the Good Friend using the proper medium under the right conditions.

"Let’s do it."

If I waited any longer, I might end up reliving that horror from the late-night talk show again. Cold sweat broke out as I quickly unlocked the motel room and stepped inside.

But then.

The moment I opened the door, someone was already inside.

Lizard head.

"Mr. Deer."

......??

The familiar lizard face I’d spent the last six days with looked at me quietly.

I nearly rubbed my eyes.

"Wh-what, Section Chief Lee Jaheon??"

I looked outside the motel room and then back in again, but no matter how many times I checked, it was really the lizard section chief.

No...!

I rushed into the room and carefully locked the door behind me.

Don’t tell me...

"Did Director Ho send you?"

"No."

"Then how did you...?"

"I infiltrated."

"......."

You can’t just... stand there like that so casually if you infiltrated...!

‘Director Ho probably already knows we’ve been in contact, but... is this really okay?’

Can you really just waltz into a spy’s hideout like this? More importantly, who just shows up unannounced in someone else’s motel room?

"Uh... would you like to sit down first?"

"Yes."

Flustered, I sat across the table from the lizard.

I was about to explain my confusion when—

"I see. But I came here because there’s something with higher priority that needs to be dealt with."

Whew.

"I’m sorry, but I have something I need to do first."

"What is it?"

"......."

Well.

Do you remember that talk show ghost story where you shelled the place and rescued me?

Strictly speaking, I’m about to re-summon the host who caused all that chaos...

Should I say it? Or would not saying it make me some kind of dangerously naive fool?

Feeling like I was about to be swept up in something again, I opened my mouth and roughly explained the situation.

Assuming he would understand that this wasn’t something I could avoid.

"I see."

Sure enough, the lizard section chief accepted it right away.

However...

"Follow the proper safety procedures for the ritual. I’ll supervise."

"......."

He clearly had no intention of leaving.

And so, with Section Chief Lee Jaheon at my side, I began the full summoning of Good Friend Brown...

‘Is this... really happening?’

I was gonna lose it.

Chapter 187

A good friend.

The preparation for the summoning ritual was already complete.

No more of that tearjerking nonsense like last time—having to make do with a substitute of a substitute at the theme park resort, like someone chewing with their gums because they’ve got no teeth.

This time, I had prepared everything exactly as I did for the very first Good Friend summoning ritual.

The necktie, the silver snake, and... most importantly, the plush toy from the theme park gift shop.

I picked up the plushie.

The plush doll with fluffy fur was, surprisingly... a pink bunny keyring-sized toy!

I bought the gift shop off the Yellow Zone—so how the hell did I end up with a rabbit instead of a yellow-horned cat?

Simple.

"Using the mascot authority, I set up a special sales booth."

[Mix and Match Your Own Plush Doll]

Custom Good Friend creation, in short.

And so, I succeeded in obtaining an original Good Friend that looked nearly identical to the one made into a keyring I got from the RealGoods box.

Well, minus the keyring components.

Now I just have to not think about what this plush toy was made from...

All is well.

Except, except....

"......"

"......"

Those eyes are burning a hole through me.

"You just follow the instructions in the manual to proceed with the summoning ritual."

"Yes."

The lizard's unblinking eyes were unbearable...

But Assistant Manager Lee Jahun showed no signs of averting ✧ NоvеIight ✧ (Original source) his gaze or compromising, so in the end, I conducted the ritual while he checked off the manual.

"It never said I had to do it alone..."

But somehow, instead of tension, I was sighing heavily as I carried it out.

This time, I drew the proper-sized circle, placed the tiny Good Friend at the center, put down the silver snake, and reenacted the ritual exactly as I did before.

"Same necktie as before..."

Holding salt in my mouth.

I lit the necktie with a lighter.

Tsssssss.

...It brought me back to when I first attempted this.

Back when I was less scared than now, when I hadn’t fully given up yet—when I frantically tried to find an escape route from the ghost story I was trapped in.

And the being that answered that ritual.

Flickering.

The burning necktie emitted flickering flames. In that light, the plush doll’s shadow trembled as if moving.

And then—

The plushie’s mouth opened.

“—Aaaaaaaaah!”

.......

Huh?

“—Stop! Stop! I got here first pleaseforgivemetherearetoomanyguestsonewhoknowsglorysir?thismethodofconsultingrockpaperscissorsyoucalledmeyoupatheticcreaturesbegone! The Mountain Lord Arriveeeeeees!”

The tiny plush doll squirmed violently as if crammed full of something. But soon—

“Oh.”

It stopped.

“Ohhhhh the Master of the Grand Performance Arrives!”

A chorus.

As if someone already held full control, leading the audience into a roar of applause.

“He has arrived! He has arrived! He has returned! He has come to see you! The one who has come to see—You!”

The plush raised both hands high.

Then...

Spoke with childlike joy.

“Wow! My friend called me again!”

"......!"

In the next moment—

Everything vanished.

The six-pointed star drawn in moisture, the necktie I was holding, the coin, the salt in my mouth.

Gone, like they were never there to begin with.

All that remained was a single plush toy sitting idly on the motel room floor.

That fluffy stuffed bunny.

“...Brown?”

But no response.

I almost panicked, but quickly understood the situation.

“Good Friend only ever spoke and moved inside the ghost story!”

And this is reality. If the plush wasn’t enhanced into its bloodthirsty form, there were certain requirements. So...

“Time to set the stage!”

I quickly propped Brown up against the bedframe, turned off the room lights, and left only one lamp on under the bed.

So that its light cast a shadow of the plush on the wall.

My hands trembled slightly as I worked.

I wasn’t sure if it was from anticipation or from whatever the hell I had just witnessed.

“...What even happened just now?”

It was like countless things were being summoned into that plushie, but it all passed too fast to analyze.

I glanced instinctively at Assistant Manager Lizard—he nodded.

“The procedure was executed safely.”

"......."

Right....

In any case, I had finally completed the preparations to converse with Good Friend in the real world, and I leaned against the bed and stared at the large shadow of the plushie on the wall...

And then—

“Friend!”

“...!”

A cheerful and familiar voice echoed in the room.

The voice of the bunny plush.

“...Brown.”

“We’ve finally met again! How touching! Oh, this would be the perfect opening for a talk show!”

“Mr. Roe, I’ve heard you’ve been through so much. But worry no more. Your most loyal advisor, your finest guide, the one and only friend in this world has returned to your side!”

That dramatic, showman tone.

Strangely, hearing that voice made something warm and emotional stir faintly in me.

“At times like this, a hug and applause are in order! Let’s cue the audience... hmm. No movement.”

“Mr. Roe, my body feels strangely waterlogged, like soaked cotton... but if I had your help, I think I could manage. Now then, shall we stand before the camera together?”

“Haha... nah, it’s fine.”

It was eerie enough that I actually felt nostalgic hearing this freaky voice again.

I leaned comfortably on the bed, watching the plush doll’s shadow on the wall.

“Let’s talk a bit more first. How’s your condition?”

“Couldn’t be better! As long as I can talk with my friend, heavy limbs are a small price to pay for peace. I’ll get used to it in time!”

“Then let us once again return to the start of joyful days with Brown...”

There was a rustling sound, like someone looking around in costume.

The voice paused for a beat.

“Oh my goodness. Friend, where on earth are you living?”

Ah.

I looked around the room.

As expected from a cheap motel behind a train station... yeah, it’s a rundown room...

“Was my body leaning against this filthy wall? My stars!”

“It’s not dirty. I cleaned everything before the ritual... it’s just old.”

It just looks dirtier because of the floral pattern!

But that wasn’t convincing enough for a talk show host.

“‘Just old’? No, this is an insult to housing. Even third-rate washed-up actors wouldn’t stay in a trailer like this—and yet my friend is living here?!”

“What happened to your humble little haven from before?”

“Well... uh. My job changed, so I couldn’t stay there anymore.”

“What a miserable company!”

Well...

Director Ho actually suggested I get an officetel for stability and proper ID cover for my undercover mission.

He even said he’d cover the cost.

But I refused.

How do I put it... I didn’t want to set up something I might start calling “home.”

The company housing felt more temporary, less like my own. But if I made a place just for myself... I was afraid I’d start letting my guard down.

“This place is actually pretty livable. I’ll take good care of your spot.”

“My god...”

“Friend, I supported your independent and proactive decision, even cheered when you quit your job—and yet you’re living in a place like this.”

The plush spoke in a sorrowful tone.

“Please don’t forget that Brown’s talk show is always open to you, friend. Though of course, you’ll need to pass a light interview first...”

“Mr. Roe, is this plush doll currently trying to tempt you into a talk-show-shaped darkness?”

“Well, I mean... it always does...”

......

......?!

I turned to the lizard.

The white lizard perched on the bedframe was calmly looking at me.

Calmly?!

“Can you hear this!?”

“Yes.”

I almost fainted.

Wait. Does that mean he’s been hearing our whole conversation up until now? Even back when I left Brown with Assistant Manager Lee and went into the ghost story? No, more importantly!

“Th-this item normally can’t be heard by others! How the hell are you able to hear it...?!”

“? The subject is clearly expressing intent to communicate.”

I stared at him, mouth agape.

“...Normal humans can’t hear it.”

“Yes.”

"It will look suspicious to others."

"I see."

The lizard just stared blankly at me, as if to say, "So what do you want me to do about it?"

"There are no others in this space."

"......."

I gave up on everything.

Come to think of it, Assistant Manager Lee Jahun recognized me even when I turned into a child, even when I wore a security team suit, even when I had a mascot head on.

‘Is he specialized in mental identity or communication...?’

It’s probably best to assume it’s one of those reptilian alien abilities.

That’s more comforting...

"Mr. Roe."

"Yes."

"Would you like some advice?"

"...Excuse me?"

"Burn the doll immediately."

–Oh.

Ugh.

"The summoning ritual was performed according to safety protocols. However, the summoned entity shows signs of being able to break free from the ritual’s restrictions at any time."

–This rude blade-wielder still doesn’t know his place, I see.

Oh please.

–If you're not even an invited guest, you should at least follow the unspoken rule of keeping your mouth shut. Such vulgar loquacity! Unbelievable—but for my dear friend’s sake, I’ll offer a few words.

–I am a being who respects my friend’s privacy and is prepared to help with anything. Because...

–I’m a good friend!

"Mr. Roe disappeared for a month because he trusted those restrictions. Don’t forget that."

–Oh, that was merely an expression of my tender concern for my friend’s safety. Thanks to it, Mr. Roe spent a blissful month in a safe, fulfilling workplace, both body and mind... At least until you dared to stomp into the great studio with your filthy feet.

"He's threatening you."

"J-just a moment."

Look, I already signed the contract—there’s no getting out of this ghost story world. There’s no need to make things worse between us!

"I said firmly that I was quitting, and in the end, he let me go, didn’t he?"

–Of course!

"What reasoning did you use to persuade him?"

That was...

"I told him I’d show him something more interesting."

–Ah, yes. I’ve been very curious to see the result of that bold promise...

–But even if it isn’t entertaining, a Good Friend will always protect you. A Good Friend doesn’t delight in their friend’s suffering, after all!

"......."

That odd tone again.

Now that I think about it, this plush version of Brown is using much warmer, more loyal-sounding language than he did during that phone call back at the theme park.

Just like before.

‘Now that he's been summoned into the Good Friend plush, maybe he’s bound by that old rule again?’

But unlike before, I could tell for sure now.

That he viewed the ritual’s restrictions more as entertainment than as limitations.

That even though the restriction had become useless, he still played along with it out of a weird sense of nostalgia and rhythm...

If he found it boring, he could break through the restrictions and drag me back to the talk show staff at any time.

‘...Thanks to Assistant Manager Lizard, I’ve regained my sense of danger.’

If I wasn't careful, I might’ve gotten sentimental and too attached to Good Friend again.

Right. I shouldn’t take everything Good Friend says at face value.

What happened last time must never repeat.

"Alright. Thanks. So... Brown, is it okay if you go back in my front pocket for now?"

–Oh, I wouldn’t refuse that!

I finally managed to change the subject and bring the conversation with Brown to a natural end.

Phew.

As soon as I tucked Brown into the front pocket of my jacket, I let out a sigh of relief.

That weird sense of stability, like things were finally back on track.

By the way, my right arm’s goblin-fire prosthetic trembled when I tried to use it to grab the plush, so I used my left hand instead.

‘Since when did it take this much negotiation just to move my own body...?’

I think I’m reaching enlightenment.

"Is it over?"

"...Yes."

Thankfully, the lizard didn’t do anything extreme like snatching Good Friend and throwing him out the window.

Though he did stare silently at the bunny plush for a while.

‘...If Brown had been able to move right now, they probably would’ve fought.’

Honestly, it was a relief that he wasn’t active.

Trying to lighten the mood, I spoke again.

"Um, Assistant Manager. If you came here today because you noticed what was going on..."

"I didn’t."

Then what?

"Then is there another reason you came?"

"Yes."

"Just a second."

Phew.

I sat down again at the table and offered some snacks and coffee to the Assistant Manager.

He accepted the coffee without hesitation, and I sat across from him with my own cup.

"I’d like to hear it now."

I was ready to talk.

...I also wondered if it might have something to do with Team D.

"Yes."

After swallowing a snack whole, wrapper and all, the lizard looked at me and said:

A topic I never expected.

"Mr. Roe, did you have a Dream Residue Collector with you during your last ‘Cheerful Theme Park!’ exploration?"

.......

Huh?

"The company didn’t give me one."

"I see."

Right. I was pretending to be from the Special Division of the Security Team.

Normally, the security team doesn’t get collectors because they’re too contaminated. The collector only gives out dream solution if it judges the user as human—which they usually don’t.

But I had brought one myself.

An elite collector.

The one I got from the RealGoods Box—it had let me extract potion materials from the dream cultivation chamber more than once.

‘That last expedition was ranked A-grade.’

Maybe I should check it now.

I opened my mouth and accessed the Elite Collector stored in my inventory tattoo.

"But why are you asking about th—"

The collector was empty.

"......."

Huh?

"Mr. Roe."

‘That’s... odd.’

I clearly cleared the Darkness and escaped. The collector should be full.

Why isn’t it?

The sentence I had just recalled:

–The collector only dispenses dream solution when it recognizes the user as human. If it can’t make that judgment, it doesn’t give anything.

.......

Wait.

It didn’t recognize me as human?

It... judged me as part of the ghost story?

But...

‘I’m still completely me.’

I didn’t do anything strange or show any abnormal behavior, like back at the Fox Counseling Room.

I thought I was maintaining my identity just fine...

"Assistant Manager."

"Yes."

"Am I... contaminated?"

I met the lizard’s eyes.

He answered.

"Yes."

......Ah.

‘That... might be possible.’

And then—

A radiant beam of light descended from above.

Instinctively, I looked up and saw a familiar notepad and message...

[Darkness Exploration Log – RealGoods Box]

–New item usage rights unlocked! (!)

A special phenomenon I hadn’t seen in a while.

I reached out automatically to catch the item falling from the box.

A small silver music box.

"......."

The item I bought at the pop-up store.

A piece of Nameless Radiance Cult merchandise.

Darkness Exploration Log / Nameless Radiance Cult

/ ITEM

Hymn Music Box

A replica of a music box obtained after a ritual performed by the Nameless Radiance Cult.

It’s said that inside the original was engraved the phrase: “To know the truth is power.”

When the melody plays, those assimilated by the ghost story have a chance of regaining human reason and identity.

However, after hearing it more than three times, they begin to spiral into madness and commit horrifying, unpredictable acts. Refer to usage logs here.

Some claim this is merely a side effect of awakening to a truth of this world no one was ever meant to know—and that the true use of the music box only begins after the third listening.

The melody disappears the moment the box is closed, leaving behind only a vague feeling—never to be recalled in the mind again.

Item usage condition: Must be an assimilated being who has been granted the Nameless Radiance Cult’s sacred scripture.

The term “assimilated being” refers to someone who’s been mutated so much they can no longer be judged as human in a ghost story.

So... the fact that I received this means—

I meet the condition for usage.

I’ve been completely contaminated by the ghost story.

And I’m aware of it.

"Mr. Roe."

"......."

"If you still don’t want to visit the Fox Counseling Room, please nod."

This is insane.

"If you’re unable to decide, initiate the emergency fallback instead."

Cell?

You mean that golden chip shaped like a SIM card?

"Place it under your tongue."

"......."

Normally, I’d hesitate and overthink all this.

"Okay."

I pressed the “cell” under my tongue.

Chapter 188

The Universal Shopping Mall.

How much had I spent there?

Even if I didn’t remember every single item I bought, it wasn’t hard to calculate that it was in the hundreds of millions of won.

I had blown an amount equivalent to a full deposit on a decent apartment in the greater Seoul area, all to buy random items from that mall.

And then, one day, I received that golden card.

I was a VIP of the Universal Shopping Mall!

With Gratitude Cell Enclosed

Back then, I had absolutely no idea what that so-called gratitude cell even meant.

Until now.

"Dear customer."

I looked up.

I remembered placing the cell under my tongue, but everything after that was blurry.

So... did I just doze off for a second?

"Dear customer."

Wait a second. Assistant Manager. I need a moment to figure out what—

Wait.

Dear customer?

"Assistant Manager?!"

"I am not."

Only then did I get a proper look at the figure in front of me.

A lizard was staring blankly at me.

"Assistant Manager."

"I am not, dear customer."

That was the second time he clearly denied it.

I looked closely again at the reptile in front of me. The scaly snout and glinting vertical pupils were the same.

But the color—was different.

‘G-grey?!’

A grey lizard was now sitting exactly where Lee Jahun had been just moments ago!

Even the snacks and coffee I’d hastily set out on the table were gone.

"Who are you?"

"I am a Shopper dispatched to act on behalf of the Universal Shopping Mall's sales representative, here to assist our VIP customer with a private shopping session."

Oh no.

"Would you like to begin your shopping?"

I stood up. I was still sitting at the same cheap motel table.

But across from me, Assistant Manager Lee had been replaced by some alien-looking being... Hold on.

VIP shopping... from the Universal Shopping Mall?

Don’t tell me... is this like an in-person sales rep showing up for a one-on-one home shopping visit?

Ah, ahhhhhh...

But Agent Choi waved me off.

He was reading the message I’d written on the board.

I played along in character as best I could.

–Pleasure to meet you! Oho, that scar on your neck has quite the flair. I do hope you'll tell the story behind it one day.

No. He just thought I was suspicious and flagged me.

–Still, not bad. A classic trio—two loyal sidekicks supporting the protagonist!

Nope. They’re my superiors.

My bosses.

And right now, both of them were looking at Brown with curious eyes.

Agent Bronze’s face turned complicated.

He was clearly remembering all the times he’d glimpsed Brown sticking out of my pocket or hanging off my clothes...

‘But if he says anything, it’ll sound like we’ve known each other for a while!’

I jumped in to spare him the awkwardness.

Thud.

–My goodness. I'd almost prefer it were a comedy skit. My friend’s been conned into a job scam.

To the eyes of a host from a glitzy, capitalist-era mass media ghost story, a public office facility from the 21st century must’ve looked like a budget failure on every level.

Standing in front of the New Investigations office—my fellow spy.

Ms. Go Yeongeun.

“......Yes.”

Now that I think about it, Yeongeun had seen this plush version of Brown quite a few times.

Even during that ghost story exhibition, The House of the Blind, she might’ve glimpsed me using Brown to set the mood with Baek Saheon.

Apparently, Brown had left more of an impression than I thought.

People in this ghost story world carry all kinds of weird crap—rabbit feet, mummy bandages, you name it.

Not to mention, everyone walks around wearing bizarre animal masks. I figured a little plush keychain wouldn’t even register.

No, I just meant—does he own a plush too...?

It’s becoming increasingly clear this rabbit is way more noticeable than I thought.

...What even is this atmosphere?
