What the hell is going on here.

On my way back home.

Brown generously gave the Bureau a passing score, and I rushed my steps toward the motel.

Right. The wristwatch.

...The geumje placed on security staff.

Daydream Inc. had decided that once a rescued staffer had recovered enough of their humanity, instead of investing further treatment, they’d just... repurpose them.

In Exploration Logs, the geumje isn’t described in detail—it was considered more eerie to leave it ambiguous.

For example...

Yeah, yeah, security team members all wore wristwatches. Why? No clue.

The sound of ticking.

Which was weird, because they were digital watches—why would you hear ticking?

And then it would speed up...

And then you’d hear a chime.

They were just... gone.

Not the watch.

The person.

—Interrogation 26, Glass Cell Inmate G56 (Origin: Daydream Inc.)

...??

Suspicious as hell.

Why is he trying to increase the scale of the disciplinary action he's going to receive?

Even if Gwak Jegang didn’t, someone else did.

Damn it.

At that point, it’s not just about them realizing I’m still alive.

Authorized Lethal Force?!

Oh, right. Security does technically have authorization to use deadly force!

No, the lethal force authorization isn’t for the security team!

And it basically means “don’t get in the security team’s way and jack up costs.”

If it were that easy to kill people, Gwak Jegang would’ve been reduced to a puddle of blood by some other security team ages ago.

I’d gotten too greedy when he offered to dig up info on Yuquae Research.

Goddamn it.

Kids' Disguise Syrup (Green Grape Flavor)

★★★★★

One of the most iconic interrogation tools of Daydream Inc.’s security team.

Exploration Log / Ghost Story

[All That Carrot Can Do]

Code: Qterw-E-2525 (Daydream Inc.)

A children’s toy that facilitates “truth games.”

If a participant lies, it immediately detects it and imposes a brutal challenge or penalty.

Failure to complete the task allows the other participant to punish the liar.

Where’d I get it?

Truth is—it’s fake.

I used the Kids’ Disguise Syrup on my smartphone to make this knockoff.

That’s why it’s got five stars.

The syrup doesn’t transform people—it disguises objects to perfectly resemble what you picture.

Which is why it has unmatched versatility.

Even a counterfeit works, as long as the target believes it’s real.

Chapter 192

Underground lab.

Gwak Jegang’s gaze trembled as he looked around the Dream Incubation Chamber of the Yuquae Research Center—at the massive central incubator.

Not fear—excitement.

What should I make him think I’m from?

At the yellow lantern hanging from my belt, coiled in bone-like ribbed horns.

This question wasn’t about the incubator.

As if.

I just knew about this bastard’s insane antics from the Exploration Log #Bonus: Team J’s Endless Life.

An irregular entry infamous for leaving readers with a foul taste in their mouths—the kind of thing that makes your stomach churn just thinking what if that actually happened.

‘There’s no way he’ll think I’m just some former employee.’

Doesn’t matter how good Gwak Jegang is at reading people—it’s impossible.

‘Ah... someone higher up must’ve noticed what I did.’

From there, it’s easy.

"Haha... if this isn’t a formal disciplinary hearing and you’re using the security team to question me off-record... then... what exactly did I tamper with? What secret did I stumble onto?"

Letting him misinterpret it works just fine. I could steer the entire situation any way I wanted.

A cheerful child’s voice rang out.

[What do you think this machine in front of you is capable of?]

“...A stronger, more fundamental potion maker! Since it came from the darkness, it must be even more extreme, governed by logic humans can’t grasp! May I touch it? No, just observe?”

I allowed him to rise, but monitored his every movement with utmost caution as he touched the incubator.

Even as cold sweat streamed down, he couldn’t contain his excitement, flitting from part to part, tapping and inspecting everything.

“It’s crude... but somehow more complex than the final company versions. That makes sense, I suppose. Fewer buttons... but emojis? What is this? And this here...”

He pressed something on the side.

...A pipe similar to the one I used to inject the distillate revealed itself.

Didn’t know that was there.

‘So that’s how you extract the distillate? Good to remember.’

If needed, I could siphon off the Dream Distillate from there.

Meanwhile, Gwak Jegang was completely engrossed, eyes glued to every piece, peering inside the glass container where the Dream Distillate was stored.

Specifically, the faint silhouette floating within it.

A crimson mass about five times the size of an egg.

"That thing in the center! What is it? That wasn’t in the company’s machines!"

I said nothing.

Because I didn’t know either....

But thanks to the mood I’d set, Gwak Jegang interpreted my silence all on his own.

He seemed to take it as “You’re not authorized to know.”

He clicked his tongue and pulled his hand back—but his gaze still roamed hungrily across the incubator.

Until it landed near the bottom.

Where a single button was missing.

◎

"Ah, this spot... it's probably for producing the wish slip."

......!

"Right? I think I’m right. Oh—did you remove the button just in case I might try pressing it?"

Hold on.

That’s where the wish slip was originally supposed to go?

So even the prototype had a function to print wish slips?

‘That means... the original wish slips were even stronger than the ones we know.’

Just what kind of monstrosity was Yuquae Research developing?

And—

‘If I locate and restore that missing button, could I get my hands on a Yuquae Research–version wish slip?’

Thump. Thump.

My head throbbed with the sound of my own heartbeat.

No. Calm down.

This was still just Gwak Jegang's speculation.

And even if it was true, all the wish slips I’ve seen so far have been refined by Daydream Inc.

No one could say what kind of absurd effect the original one might have had.

Stick to what’s real. Don’t get seduced by long shots.

Hoo.

“If you gave me a little more time, I think I could figure out a lot more. Whoever you are, if you’d let me join this project...”

Gwak Jegang made his pitch smoothly, eyeing me.

I stared silently.

A strand of black smoke slithered toward him.

He flinched.

The smoke curled around him—less a threat, more an evaluation—and as I inhaled, it drew back into my body.

"Hah."

I reached out and turned on the Carrot Recorder again.

Time to wrap this up.

‘Push your luck too far and he might catch on.’

And the final blow—was to seal the narrative perfectly.

[In today’s game of truth, what was the one thing you didn’t want anyone to know?]

“...!”

A staple final question in security team interrogations.

A direct hit—no matter how you danced around the truth earlier.

But Gwak Jegang only grinned.

“Nothing at all. Haha!”

He was absolutely insane.

“You already know about the thing I tried to hide, and you’re letting it slide. What else would I hide?”

He smacked his lips.

“It’s just a shame, really. Guess I’m not what your team is looking for. Hm... then who is on that team? Confidential ops, disappearances, hmm.”

And then—

“Kim Soleum, maybe?”

I nearly jumped out of my seat.

“Now that I think about it, the build's about right... And yeah, Kim Soleum's mask had horns too, didn’t it? Did you hijack that staffer on the train and use them as a vessel?”

You bastard.

“Something about that whole disappearance felt off! That ghost story wasn’t the type where people just vanish like that—this finally puts the puzzle together! Hahaha!”

Hold on.

He said that ghost story wasn’t the kind where people vanish.

Like he already knew about the Tamna-bound ghost train?

In that moment, it clicked.

This bastard planned the whole thing.

The sudden out-of-town assignment. Getting tangled up in the ghost train on the way.

It wasn’t a string of unlucky coincidences. It was all laid out by this freak.

‘He had advance data on the Tamna ghost story.’

Right. Gwak Jegang could manipulate ghost stories in ways that never made it into the official Exploration Logs.

Because of me. Because of the variable named "me."

“A morally upright rookie who knows fear... really, just listing the traits, sounds like a perfect sacrificial offering, doesn't it?”

That tone—like he’d done a full background check on me.

...He knew?

Knew what I was like?

"Anyway. Now I really want to confirm my hypothesis. That security uniform—was it made from that unfortunate employee's skin? Did it match your resonance?"

Stay calm.

I gave no reaction.

Whether he spouted nonsense or stumbled onto the truth—I gave him nothing.

I had no interest in anything outside the task at hand.

Then, casually, I picked up the Carrot Recorder and gave it a small shake.

"......Tch. So stoic...."

And then—

The Security Captain lunged from behind the desk and struck the back of Gwak Jegang’s neck, knocking him out cold.

“Mmgh.”

Thump—he collapsed to the floor with a short grunt.

Hoo.

Oddly satisfying.

“Outside... okay, right...?”

I nodded quickly.

As the captain began to drag Gwak Jegang out, I hurried to strip off the security uniform.

...Done.

So what now?

The security captain would dump Gwak Jegang near the Daydream HQ.

And he’d be ‘found’ by Assistant Manager Park Minseong and returned to the company.

That way, Minseong could officially report that he discovered Gwak Jegang unconscious near the premises.

That would be the last sighting.

Meaning his interaction with me—or the captain—never officially happened.

So Park Minseong wouldn’t have to lie to leave me out of the report.

-Mr. Deer! Oh, that was delightful to watch. Deception, misdirection, secrecy—three of my favorite things, brilliantly executed!

After changing back, I picked up Brown, who had been seated at the edge of the room, and tucked him into my front pocket.

He burst into applause.

‘Really? It didn’t come off weird?’

-Oh, seamless! That unflinching calm even when your own name came up—simply superb! A marvelous performance.

-But... aren’t you worried? That man might go back and spill everything.

Oh, that?

I grinned.

‘Not a chance.’

-Oho?

Gwak Jegang was a smart man.

Security interrogations came with a natural code of silence.

Unless someone directly involved asked him about it, he’d keep his mouth shut.

Especially in his case.

If he spilled that the whole thing was about his own gross misconduct?

He’d only be hurting himself.

Especially before he’d even had the chance to observe the fallout.

‘He’ll keep quiet just to see what happens.’

-I see!

This was better than some corporate gag order.

I remembered what Park Minseong had said, worried.

-If only we had some memory-wiping potion... or a truth seal or something.

I could’ve bought those.

But I didn’t.

Because those are Daydream-made potions.

Someone like Gwak Jegang would know how to undo them. He could buy the antidote from the point store.

If I used something like that, he’d grow suspicious.

The important thing was to make this feel natural.

Like there was no point in talking.

And even if, somehow, he did talk—

It would still sound like some faction’s top-secret project, and no one would be able to tell for sure.

-So Mr. Deer went all-in on perfectly recreating what a ‘Security Team interrogation’ would feel like.

-Even if the story spreads, the people involved will all be fooled.

‘Exactly.’

Only thing that bothered me was... that bit where he said Kim Soleum’s build looked similar to mine.

But even if Gwak Jegang went around blabbing with that level of detail—

By the time it reached Director Ho’s ears, I’d be long gone.

“That’s that.”

I’d squeezed Gwak Jegang dry and managed to cover everything.

My whole body ached....

Maybe I’ll rest a bit before the captain gets back.

Dealing with that lunatic researcher left me drained.

I stowed the security uniform back into my inventory tattoo and sat down in front of the dream incubator.

Then quietly stared at it.

-Sorry to disturb your break, Mr. Deer—but ah, I simply can’t resist giving you a teaser.

‘...A teaser?’

-That’s right! Let’s heighten the suspense just a touch.

-Something very fun is about to happen.

“...Brown?”

That’s when it happened.

Bzzzzt.

My phone buzzed.

“...!”

A call? At this hour?

Did something happen at the Disaster Management Bureau?

Maybe a double ping of overlapping SOS requests?

I immediately checked my screen.

And then—

[K.LEE: Hey long time no seeee lololol]

[K.LEE: How’s life been?]

What?

Hawaiian beach pic for the profile photo.

The guy who introduced himself as Assistant Manager Lee Kangheon from Team C, the one who’d connected me to Salmon Market.

But...

He texted me?

That shouldn’t be possible.

Because, well—

I’m supposed to be dead to Daydream.

And I changed my number long ago.

‘.......’

Something’s wrong.

And the phone kept buzzing.

[K.LEE: Oh yeah lol heard you’re a manager now]

[K.LEE: Wait...]

[K.LEE: Are you a spy?]

“...Really now.”

I turned.

Behind an office chair in the dream incubator lab, someone pulled off a goblin mask.

Agent Choi.

Holding a crumpled note—the one I had handed him.

“You’re gonna be a spy?”

His eyes locked on mine.

“If you were serious about that... you should’ve made sure this little clue disappeared somewhere along the way.”

“...Mr. Deer.”

The axe came down.

Chapter 193

I rolled to avoid the descending guillotine blade.

But one had already pierced my foot.

“...!”

At that moment, Agent Choi lunged toward me with terrifying speed, pulling something from his coat.

A rounded object.

A glass bead catching the light of the dream-solution glow.

A portable Glass Prison.

The instant that hit me, I’d be restrained and transferred to the infamous Glass Prison of the Paranormal Disaster Control Bureau.

And with it—the end of everything.

The spy mission. The wish token.

“No.”

Gritting my teeth, I yanked the guillotine from my foot and rolled sideways.

Ting, tiding...

The glass bead I barely dodged hit the floor with a cheerful clink.

But the prison had already activated.

“...!”

The convict—

Is called—

To receive chains.

From the glassy void, countless hands emerged to seize me.

I clung to the edge of the desk, but it was no use. I was dragged in. Fingernails shattered as my hand scraped the floor.

No time to reach for an item.

“Brown!”

–Oh, my dear friend is desperately calling for my devoted assistance!

–But I’m afraid it won’t be possible.

What?!

–This is all for a more thrilling development, so I know you’ll understand, my friend. Ah, but it breaks my heart! It really does!

–Your suffering wounds me!

My blood ran cold.

And then—I was trapped in glass.

Chara-rang...

The glass resonated with a clear, sharp chime. Like gazing through a cracked mirror, I was isolated from the outside.

“Brown?”

No answer. Fuck!

No.

I clenched my jaw.

If the agent contacts the Bureau now, it’s all over.

And Agent Choi is exactly the kind of guy who would.

“He’s already starting.”

In his hand, the glass bead spun—an impromptu ritual to summon the Glass Prison’s warden.

“Damn it. Damn it!”

How do I stop this? How do I talk him down? He’s a principled guy, sure, and sometimes his emotions get the better of him, but only when there’s a crack to slip through.

Now that he knows I’m a spy, his first move will be to lock me up and then ask questions—

Wait.

Instead of looking at the glass bead in Agent Choi’s right hand, I turned to his left.

The crumpled slip of paper.

...

Found it.

But I didn’t give up.

“No.”

“...!”

“I figured it was a setup. Given your background.”

...!!

Of course.

It had felt off.

Back when K. Lee from Team C didn’t exist, and when Assistant Manager Lee Seong-hae claimed never to have met me.

There was a simple conclusion—

They never existed in the first place.

Someone had been impersonating Daydream staff to extract information.

That person.

They’d known from the start who I was.

...!

Agent Choi’s eyes darkened.

“Pointless, right?”

A voice came from above his head.

“...!”

A massive beast, like a wolf, jagged teeth and glowing eyes—

The Security Captain.

He was back.

The jaws closed.

CRACK.

The guillotine shattered. The glass bead was crushed in his hand.

The prison shattered too.

I burst free immediately.

Then charged into the opening—and tackled Agent Choi.

Aiming for the slowest-responding point.

His neck.

I intentionally targeted his scar.

A jolt of guilt or nerves surged down my spine.

Thud.

From the wolf-beast’s shadowy muzzle, teeth and a tongue-like mass emerged, preparing to rip into his arm—

“Don’t.”

I gasped out the words.

“Don’t kill him.”

And there stood the hollow-eyed Security Captain again.

“...Wasn’t gonna... kill him... just scare him a bit....”

Whew.

“That kind of scare tactic won’t work on someone like him.”

I looked down at Agent Choi.

My heart pounded.

Now what?

Was my mission totally blown?

What about the wish token?

How do I recover from this?

How do I—

“Soleum.”

...!

I had seen it.

In the Shining Shimmering Dragon Palace ghost story.

The moment Assistant Manager Jin Nasol collapsed with a stifled groan the instant the blade was driven into him.

Agent Choi’s gaze was calm, piercingly clear.

“I’ll help you, Soleum.”

.......

I moved my hand.

“Agent-nim.”

Clatter.

From beneath his left elbow, the emergency call device fell. I snatched it and crushed it in my hand.

“You—!”

Exactly as expected.

“Agent Choi.”

Pressing both his arms firmly behind his back, I declared clearly,

“I know you.”

“.......”

The security captain did as I asked.

Agent Choi paled when he saw the escape item in my hand.

I couldn’t answer.

Because this was all meant to stop him from doing anything reckless—to instill that sense of danger.

I confiscated all his equipment one by one, then stored them in my inventory tattoo.

That finished the first round of neutralization.

Huff...

But I didn’t feel safe at all. My mind was racing.

We need to get out of here.

Staying with Agent Choi in this place was too risky. If the Bureau tracked this place, it was over.

Motel.

We had to move to a neutral location for now.

I stood Agent Choi up and held onto him. With the Security Captain supporting from the other side, it looked—for all the world—like we were helping a drunk coworker home.

“Soleum.”

I couldn’t say it.

And—I knew he wouldn’t help.

He was just probing for a moment of hesitation—a single crack in my resolve.

And the moment I wavered, this experienced agent would either drag me to the Glass Prison or escape and return with reinforcements.

That would be the end. Or rather—

It’s already over, isn’t it?

A hollow nausea rose in my gut.

The feeling that I’d passed the point of no return.

What I thought would be a simple job—quietly retrieving a document at Director Ho’s request—was now never coming back.

I was slipping into a swamp I couldn’t escape.

I wanted to throw up.

What do I do?

What options were left?

The memory-erasing or vow-sealing potions from Daydream wouldn’t work.

He’d get caught in under a week.

Agent Choi had a self-imposed weekly medical check to verify he wasn’t infected or tampered with. It’d show up there.

So maybe, maybe—

“Um....”

“This guy... he’s from the Bureau?”

Something seemed to dawn on him.

“You guys... seemed to know each other? You were talking, at least.”

But what could I even say?

I couldn’t talk about what kind of spy work I was doing, or exactly what Daydream wanted from me—those were the core truths.

Director Ho’s Geumje bound me.

Without breaking that, the only thing I could talk about was...

Not the spy. Just me.

Kim Soleum.

What I wanted.

What I thought while working for the Disaster Bureau.

Whether or not I ever intended to deceive them.

He wouldn’t dare make a move with a civilian present. He’d worry the cab driver might get hurt or even die.

If anything, it would help prevent an outburst.

“Soleum-nim!”

Director Ho.

What the hell is this.

Because Director Ho announced—

“Oh dear, I see you’ve been discovered.”

No.

His gaze moved past the Security Captain as if he weren’t even there.

Agent Choi—sat in enforced silence.

Not a human.

Something that had always shown hatred and hostility toward the Bureau stared down one of its agents.

A smile also bloomed across Director Ho’s face.

No.

I felt chills from my scalp to my toes.

Above Agent Choi’s face, a perfect ripple began to spread.

Director Ho’s incantation.

Chapter 194

Director Ho’s mouth did not open.

But the incantation had begun.

—Male.

Rippling circular indentations shimmered across Choi Agent’s face.

The smile on the agent’s face warped along with the waves.

And the moment he recoiled—

Thud.

“...!!”

Blood poured as if one side of his face had collapsed.

Chunks of flesh were torn away in the exact pattern of the ripples.

No.

“Don’t move. You mustn’t move.”

I held onto Choi Agent.

‘It’s not on the back of his hand—it’s on his face.’

This was a different treatment than for spies.

It meant, “If you move, you’ll be killed.”

Cold sweat ran down my neck.

The incantation continued.

—You will never be able to speak of this matter.

That meant—

—You will become incapable of disclosing it.

The bleeding intensified.

Blood flowed over my hand where I was gripping Choi Agent’s shoulder.

What can I do?

Director Ho’s incantation doesn’t come from his mouth. Even if I tried to talk to him or plead, I couldn’t stop it.

—Your final appearance will be as follows.

At this rate, wasn’t I just making it easier and faster for Choi Agent to die by holding him in place?

I couldn’t help him escape. The incantation had already started, and his equipment... his equipment was all sealed inside my tattoo.

‘If only I hadn’t disarmed him in the first place.’

No, if only I hadn’t given him that note to begin with.

In the end, even being transferred to the glass prison would’ve been better.

But all those moments had already passed, and now Director Ho was speaking.

—Careless hints, changes in demeanor, providing clues—these are fundamentally prohibited. Your restriction of free will will never be outwardly visible.

—All attempts to reveal the existence of the prohibition will be thwarted, and you will appear no different than before.

—Any effort to alter this will result in punishment.

“......”

Is that the end?

That’s the final clause, isn’t it? Then this ends with only the imposition of a powerful prohibition?

For a moment, I felt relief.

If I could just get through this, maybe I could find another way. At this level, perhaps....

—And tonight.

The incantation wasn’t over.

—You will come to the decision to die.

No.

—Resigned to your fate, you will take your own life in the most wretched corner of this alley...

I lunged.

At the same time, I kicked the backrest of Director Ho’s seat and reached out to grab him by the collar.

Then I slammed him down hard against the steering wheel.

BEEEEEEEEEEP!

The weight triggered the horn to scream like mad.

That sound drowned out the incantation, and the shattered focus disrupted the concentration.

The oppressive pressure that had filled the space disappeared.

In an instant, the circular ripples vanished from Choi Agent’s face.

Panting.

Silence.

...Stillness.

“......”

“......”

“Soleum-nim.”

“......”

“Did you just attack me?”

I released my hand.

My mouth barely moved.

“You had to stop.”

“......”

“It was the wrong choice.”

A reckless choice.

I had just rebuked someone—no, something—that wasn’t human, that was an incomprehensible force capable of killing me at any time, that had placed a prohibition on me, and was infinitely my superior.

“That so?”

Director Ho clapped his palms together with a crisp smack.

“You must know better than I do!”

“......”

“You couldn’t even survive a single quarter of infiltration without getting your cover blown by a teammate superior, and without giving me so much as a whisper, you went to meet the Security Team on your own as a spy!”

I’m going to die.

One wrong step here, and I’ll be dead.

Never before had Director Ho shown such open hostility. I’m going to die.

But, still—

Even so.

“That was your mistake.”

......

“...What?”

“The setup was fundamentally too sloppy. A spy should never have been deployed under their real name.”

That’s right.

“If you assumed the Disaster Response Agency wouldn’t track new recruits’ backgrounds, then that was an overly naïve decision as a planner.”

Words I couldn’t believe were coming from my mouth—words directed at someone vastly my superior—poured out.

I couldn’t stop.

“Why did you send me in under such easily exposed conditions? I thought there were backup measures in place, but there weren’t. You deliberately...”

......

Wait.

“I see.”

I looked at Director Ho.

“You did it on purpose. You wanted me to be exposed.”

“......!”

“You sent me in expecting to be caught. That’s why you had me use my real name. That’s why you only picked rookies, not veterans.”

Hoping that the inexperienced would make mistakes.

Using the easy excuse of “to bypass the Disaster Response Agency’s detection protocols,” you picked only those who were good-natured and sincere—those most suited to the agency’s image.

And there’s another added effect to this.

“The agents who uncover the identities of those infiltrators will be thrown into chaos. Because in truth, there’s no personality difference from real agents.”

Inside, there will be massive disagreements over how to handle the infiltrators.

And once they realize that no personality test can weed out spies, suspicion will spread among them.

And so—

“You orchestrated internal division within the Disaster Response Agency?”

“......”

“To slowly unravel a tightly-bonded organization, to sow seeds of distrust toward every new recruit?”

“Soleum-nim. You have quite the imagination!”

Is that so?

“But you’re not outright denying it, are you?”

A beat.

Director Ho stared at me without a word.

“‘I had no such intention, and I can swear on the Counseling Room that this is true.’ ...Can you declare that?”

“......”

“I asked, can you declare it?”

“Soleum-nim.”

A soft, kindly smile rose on Director Ho’s face.

“Even if I did, it wouldn’t change anything.”

“......!”

“There was only ever one promise from the beginning.”

Director Ho raised his index finger.

“If Soleum-nim brings me the documents I asked for, a wish ticket will be granted. That condition is an unshakably guaranteed promise.”

“So in other words.”

My mouth was dry from tension.

My heart pounded like mad.

But my lips moved without hesitation.

“Whether I’m exposed or not doesn’t really matter. As long as I can retrieve the ‘documents.’”

“......”

“As long as you get that information, Director.”

Information about an extinction-level supernatural disaster.

.......

Right.

“In that case, I’ll bring it to you no matter what.”

“The documents?”

“The information you want.”

“But you’ve already been exposed.”

“Even so,”

I met Director Ho’s gaze.

“I can retrieve it.”

Then, while gesturing toward Choi Agent, I subtly shifted to shield him with my back.

“But if that agent dies here or becomes incapacitated, the task becomes impossible.”

“And why is that?”

“Because from that point on, I won’t be able to contain it.”

“......”

“......”

Director Ho continued to look at me, smiling silently.

I didn’t break eye contact either. I held my ground.

And then—

“Go ahead.”

Director Ho declared.

“Our agreement hasn’t changed, after all. I’m really looking forward to seeing exactly how Soleum-nim will bring me those documents.”

“......”

As relief finally managed to crawl down my throat—

“But... punishment must be dealt, no?”

......!

“I trust you already understand that your behavior just now was outrageously disrespectful and inappropriate.”

My eyelids trembled.

“To begin with... none of the others were exposed. I don’t see how you can claim it wasn’t your personal fault.”

A chilling sense of doom ran down my spine.

“Excuses are your right, but to attack your superior just because your own opinion wasn’t accepted... that was a bit much, don’t you think? So if I had to guess why you went that far...”

“......”

“Did you grow attached?”

Cold sweat slid down my jaw.

“Were you scared to watch a fellow agent die? You seem to form precious attachments quite easily, even when dropped into unfamiliar environments, Soleum-nim.”

“Director.”

I answered as evenly as I could.

“Being afraid of watching someone else die is a natural human reaction.”

“......”

“If my objection offended you, I apologize.”

“Not at all! I wasn’t offended. It’s an entirely reasonable thing to say.”

A smile once again spread across Director Ho’s face.

“But rules are rules.”

“......”

“Please, show me the back of your hand. You need to be punished.”

......

I slowly extended both hands, just barely managing not to tremble.

With the backs facing upward.

—Male.

The ripple began.

But it wasn’t calm, like the secret prohibition he had cast before.

A massive, deep indentation twisted violently across the back of my hand, bringing with it a grotesque pain and distorted sensation.

“...!”

I clenched my teeth and broke out in cold sweat. I couldn’t move. If I moved....

—You will die one month from now.

...!

—However, if the agreement with ■■■ is fulfilled, the obligation will be lifted.

—Any attempt to circumvent it will result in punishment.

I held back a scream.

As the ripple on the back of my hand subsided, the twisted pain and sensation slowly faded away....

“I was worried you might’ve lied just to buy time. So this time, I decided not to be naïve about it!”

Director Ho beamed brightly.

“Thank you for letting me know.”

“......”

I withdrew my hands.

And at that moment, I locked eyes with Choi Agent’s trembling pupils.

“Ah. I think I’d better get going now.”

“......”

“I had planned to wrap things up quickly, but I didn’t expect the chat with Soleum-nim to drag on like this.”

“Chat... not really what that was....”

......!!

“You said... he’d kill me in a month....”

Captain of Security!

‘Don’t say anything—please!’

For the first time, Director Ho seemed to acknowledge the Guard Captain’s presence and turned to look at him.

Then... smiled and said:

“Give my regards to Director Cheong.”

......!

As if he hadn’t heard what the Guard Captain just said, Director Ho turned his head back again.

He looked at me and flashed a wide smile.

“Then, have a lovely evening, Soleum-nim!”

And then—

Director Ho vanished from the driver’s seat.

“......”

“......”

“I... don’t have any reason to meet Director Cheong.... Ah, he’s gone.”

I survived.

I almost collapsed onto the taxi floor.

But then I realized I was already sitting, and immediately forced my trembling body into motion.

Choi Agent’s bleeding had become gruesome.

To stop it, I opened one of the healing kits I had confiscated from his equipment and began emergency treatment.

“......”

The bleeding from his face finally stopped, and some stability returned.

Choi Agent didn’t resist.

But he still didn’t speak.

“Agent.”

He only turned his eyes to look at me.

“Don’t think.”

“Think what.”

“A way to undo the Director’s prohibition. Don’t even attempt it, don’t plan for it.”

“......”

The first incantation Director Ho had spoken had already taken effect on Choi Agent.

Now he would be incapable of conveying any information about me being a spy, or about what happened today.

He would have to behave as though today were no different from yesterday.

Otherwise—

“You’ll be punished.”

“Punished.”

“Yes.”

I lifted my head and said it directly.

“The thing you hold most dear will meet the Director.”

“......”

Choi Agent silently took back his own medical tools from my hands and continued treating himself.

The practiced skill of someone who had done this job for a long time.

And then...

“...You said ‘Director.’”

I didn’t answer.

“What kind of prohibition did the Director put on you?”

“......”

“Right now it sounds like if you don’t succeed in your spy work within a month, he’ll kill you. What did he place on you before that?”

I opened the car door.

And gestured with my chin.

“Go home.”

“How are you planning to steal the information?”

“That’s none of your concern.”

Choi Agent looked at me with an unreadable expression.

“If you tell me what the info is, I might be able to help.”

“That won’t be necessary. I won’t ask you to smuggle anything out.”

“What’s the wish you’re trying to make come true with all this?”

I couldn’t hold it in.

“To go home.”

“......!”

“...I’ll leave your equipment in the trunk.”

I exited the vehicle with the Guard Captain.

Then I retrieved all of Choi Agent’s gear and placed it in the trunk, leaving one low-grade Baekilmong regeneration potion among them.

Because if he showed up tomorrow looking like that, I couldn’t imagine the kinds of things he’d hear.

...During all this, Choi Agent never came out of the car.

Click.

I shut the trunk roughly, and began walking away with the Guard Captain.

...To somewhere.

“......”

“Um... you okay...?”

“......Yes.”

The worst was avoided.

Clearly, I had made it through.

One month.

That was enough. I could do it. I had a plan....

I could do it.

“Hmm...”

The Guard Captain’s slow voice spoke.

“If it really doesn’t work out... come find me....”

“...Thank you.”

Even just the words meant a lot.

I finally came to my senses.

“I... I’m sorry. You always help me out, and I never manage to repay you.”

Was there a bakery nearby? Even if not donuts, I wanted to give him something. But the Guard Captain slowly shook his head.

“Hm. Next time... somewhere tasty....”

“...Yes. Next time. For sure.”

A small laugh escaped me.

...Somehow, my heart eased a little.

“Um... what about the intel I found...?”

“I’ll hear it next time, too.”

But that was my limit.

With a faint smile, I saw the Guard Captain off.

He glanced back at me several times, but in the end, returned to Baekilmong before sunset....

“......”

—Phew, what a suffocating suspense sequence that was!

I lowered my head.

The plush doll on my chest came into view.

—Now, where will your journey go in this new phase? I’m quite looking forward to it. Don’t you agree, my friend?

.......

“Think whatever you want.”

I pulled the plush doll free.

I felt a fleeting impulse to throw it out onto the main road, but instead, I shoved it back into my jacket pocket.

At least this way, I wouldn’t hear its voice.

At least for today.

“......”

I returned to the shabby motel I’d been long-term renting.

And five hours later, I went to work at the Disaster Response Agency.

As if nothing had changed.

Chapter 195

“......”

I looked at the door to Hyunmu Team 1’s break room.

After everything I’d been through, it felt strangely unreal to still be standing here.

But habit was honest.

Click.

I opened the door and stepped inside.

And—

“You’re here?”

I saw Agent Cheongdong stand up with a warm face as he looked at me.

“Good morning... Agent Podo. You don’t look well.”

Ah.

“Did you have trouble sleeping again?”

“Just, a little restless, that’s all. I’m fine.”

Fortunately, I had already treated the more noticeable injuries in advance.

“If you’re too tired, please let us know.”

“...Yes. Thank you.”

I succeeded in chatting with Agent Cheongdong as usual, smiling naturally, and sat down on the sofa across from him.

Then I waited.

“......”

My palms were sweating.

But even after several minutes, the only ones in the break room were the two of us.

The person who was supposed to appear... didn’t show up.

“Hey.”

“Yes?”

“Ah. He won’t be working today.”

What?

‘Wait a second.’

Did something go wrong?

‘No.’

Don’t tell me Director Ho went to see him again. Or he tried to undo the prohibition and got caught in the process? I—

“He came in early, but said he wasn’t feeling well and submitted leave.”

There was a witness.

The feeling that swept through my chest was too strong to call relief.

Agent Cheongdong walked over to the small fridge in the corner of the break room and took something out.

A carrier of café drinks.

“He left this behind. Said he stopped by a café.”

“......”

And tucked into the cup holder was a wrapped café cookie, the kind you’d expect from a chain, and across the wrapper was a faint black marker trace.

A handwritten message from Agent Choi:

[Hyunmu Team 1 Fighting]

—with a line drawn through it.

“......”

“Agent Podo?”
